6B. Minimal loader pseudocode — Static Spiral DNA
-------------------------------------------------

Pseudo-code:

import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F

dna = np.load("results/mission_2_4_spiral_dna.npz", allow_pickle=True)
mus = torch.tensor(dna["mus"], dtype=torch.float32)  # (NL, D)
Vs = torch.tensor(dna["Vs"], dtype=torch.float32)    # (NL, R, D)

# config from meta or external JSON:
# ZOOM_LEVELS, RANK_PER_LEVEL, soft

def spiral_reconstruct(delta, mu, V, zoom_levels, rank_per_level):
    fd = delta.reshape(-1, delta.shape[-1])
    recon = fd.new_zeros(fd.shape) + mu
    current = fd - mu
    for s in range(0, V.shape[0], rank_per_level):
        Vz = V[s:s + rank_per_level]
        if Vz.abs().max().item() == 0:
            continue
        proj = (current @ Vz.T) @ Vz
        recon = recon + proj
        current = current - proj
    return recon.reshape(delta.shape)

def hook(layer_idx):
    def fn(module, inputs, output):
        x = inputs[0].float()
        delta_orig = output.float() - x
        delta_dna = spiral_reconstruct(
            delta_orig,
            mus[layer_idx].to(x.device),
            Vs[layer_idx].to(x.device),
            zoom_levels=ZOOM_LEVELS,
            rank_per_level=RANK_PER_LEVEL
        )
        delta_used = soft * delta_orig + (1 - soft) * delta_dna
        return (x + delta_used).to(output.dtype)
    return fn

# register hooks on model.model.layers[l]
# set soft=0.50 for Soft DNA

6C. Minimal loader pseudocode — Dynamic DNA
-------------------------------------------

Pseudo-code:

import numpy as np
import torch

bases = np.load("results/mission_3_2_dynamic_dna_bases.npz", allow_pickle=True)
mus = torch.tensor(bases["mus"], dtype=torch.float32)       # (NL, D)
Vs = torch.tensor(bases["Vs"], dtype=torch.float32)         # (NL, R, D)
scales = torch.tensor(bases["scales"], dtype=torch.float32) # (NL, R)

ckpt = torch.load("results/mission_3_2_dynamic_hyper.pt", map_location="cpu")
cfg = ckpt["config"]
H = DynamicDNAHyper(
    num_layers=cfg["num_layers"],
    hidden_dim=cfg["hidden"],
    total_rank=cfg["total_rank"],
    layer_embed_dim=cfg["hyper_embed"],
    hyper_hidden=cfg["hyper_hidden"],
)
H.load_state_dict({k: v.float() for k, v in ckpt["state_dict"].items()})
H.eval()

gamma = cfg.get("gamma_eval", 0.06)

def dynamic_hook(layer_idx):
    def fn(module, inputs, output):
        x = inputs[0].float()
        delta_orig = output.float() - x
        B, L, D = x.shape
        xf = x.reshape(-1, D)
        layer_ids = torch.full((xf.shape[0],), layer_idx, dtype=torch.long, device=xf.device)

        with torch.no_grad():
            alpha = H(xf, layer_ids)
            alpha = torch.nan_to_num(alpha, nan=0.0, posinf=4.0, neginf=-4.0)
            alpha = torch.clamp(alpha, -4.0, 4.0)

        mu = mus[layer_idx].to(x.device)
        V = Vs[layer_idx].to(x.device)
        scale = scales[layer_idx].to(x.device)

        delta_dna = mu + (alpha * scale) @ V
        delta_used = delta_orig + gamma * (delta_dna - delta_orig)
        return (x + delta_used.reshape(B, L, D)).to(output.dtype)
    return fn

====================================================================
SECTION 7: WHAT REMAINS UNPROVEN
====================================================================

7A. Strictly unproven claims
----------------------------

1. “Spiral Zoom is better than flat SVD at equal total rank.”
   Status: NOT_PROVEN.
   Missing: Flat Rank-12 vs Spiral 4+4+4 same-data control.

2. “Spiral Zoom generalizes to held-out entities.”
   Status: NOT_PROVEN.
   Missing: held-out pairs such as Brazil-Germany, India-China, Gold-Silver if not in calibration.

3. “Soft DNA + LoRA is the specific cause of stable generation.”
   Status: NOT_PROVEN.
   Missing: LoRA-only control, Soft-DNA-only metric control, fixed seed, broader benchmark.

4. “Dynamic DNA can replace static DNA.”
   Status: NOT_PROVEN.
   Current logs show mechanism works but output unstable.

5. “1MB DNA can run a full LLM without base model.”
   Status: NOT_PROVEN and currently implausible for general LLM because embeddings/vocab/LM-head alone are large.

6. “Fourier weight-space DNA is viable.”
   Status: NOT_SUPPORTED by current logs.
   Missing broader baselines, but current evidence is negative.

7. “Nonlinear cliff fully explains generation drift.”
   Status: PARTIALLY_SUPPORTED.
   Missing top-3 distributions, entropy, hidden-space controls, seed repetition.

7B. What is reasonably supported
--------------------------------

1. Activation-space compression behaves differently from weight-space compression.
2. Flat high-variance SVD can collapse close entity identities.
3. Hierarchical residual/Spiral Zoom can improve in-sample offline identity ratios.
4. Soft blending is more stable than hard replacement in observed generations.
5. Tiny LoRA distillation with DNA active can recover some factual anchors in short generations.
6. Dynamic HyperNet can produce input-conditioned coefficient outputs at sub-1MB artifact size.
7. Fourier coordinate weight generation failed on tested real Qwen weight matrices.

7C. Required next experiments before strong claims
--------------------------------------------------

Experiment 1 — Equal-rank Spiral control:
- Same calibration delta matrix.
- Flat Rank-12.
- Spiral 4+4+4.
- Print n, effective_rank.
- Evaluate in-sample and held-out identity pairs.

Experiment 2 — LoRA ablation matrix:
- Original
- LoRA only
- DNA only soft=0.5
- DNA + LoRA
- Hard DNA + LoRA
- same seeds, same steps, same metric suite.

Experiment 3 — Behavioral benchmark:
- 50+ prompts.
- entity_hit_continuation only after prompt.
- prefix match.
- repetition.
- gibberish.
- top-K KL.
- hidden normalized MSE.
- identity ratio for held-out pairs.

Experiment 4 — Rollout/scheduled sampling:
- Train not only teacher-forced sequences but also model-generated prefixes.
- Required for autoregressive drift.

Experiment 5 — Size-quality curve:
- total_rank = 16, 32, 64, 96, 128, 192.
- measure DNA MB vs behavioral score.
- find minimum viable DNA size.

Experiment 6 — Fourier sanity baselines:
- zero matrix
- mean matrix
- random rank-matched
- shuffled rows/cols
- larger Fourier hidden/freqs
before fully closing Fourier weight-space path.

====================================================================
FINAL COPY-PASTE VERDICT FOR OTHER AI
====================================================================

Do not accept the following as proven:
- “Spiral Zoom proves hierarchical compression beats flat SVD.”
  Missing equal-rank control.
- “LoRA distillation proves DNA stabilization.”
  Missing LoRA-only and DNA-only metric ablations.
- “1MB DNA replaces LLM.”
  Not supported.
- “Fourier DNA can generate LLM weights.”
  Current logs show failure.

Accept the following as supported evidence:
- There are concrete logs showing flat/identity-reinforced methods collapse Japan-Mexico to ratio ~0.003–0.004.
- There are concrete logs showing Spiral Zoom 4+4+4 improves in-sample Japan-Mexico ratio to 0.803.
- There are concrete logs showing Soft DNA 0.50 is more coherent than Hard DNA on Qwen2.5-1.5B.
- There are concrete logs showing LoRA + Soft DNA can produce “Tokyo”, “Mexico City/Mexican”, and “Ag” in short generations.
- There are concrete logs showing Dynamic HyperNet at ~0.78MB produces prompt-dependent outputs, but not stable generation.
- There are concrete logs showing Fourier weight-space reconstruction fails versus SVD on real q_proj weights.

The scientifically correct current statement is:

“Reverse DNA has produced promising activation-space evidence: hierarchical residual/Spiral Zoom can improve in-sample identity preservation, and Soft DNA plus tiny LoRA can stabilize short factual generations. However, equal-rank controls, held-out entity benchmarks, LoRA-only ablations, rollout training, and a size-quality curve are still required before claiming proven behavioral compression. The original 1MB standalone LLM replacement claim remains unproven.”You are an expert AI researcher, LLM compression engineer, and startup product builder.

PROJECT NAME:
Reverse DNA — Activation-Space Behavioral Compression for LLMs

CORE OBJECTIVE:
Take a pretrained LLM (initially Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct, later Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct)
and extract its task behavior/knowledge into a small "DNA" artifact made of activation-space
basis vectors, dynamic coefficient generators, and optional tiny LoRA adapters.

IMPORTANT HONEST SCOPE:
The original dream was:
"Delete the 1.8GB/3GB model and run everything from a ~1MB .dna file."

After extensive experiments, the proven realistic version is:
"Keep the base LLM, but load a small DNA cartridge + tiny adapter that steers the base model's
activation geometry so it behaves like a specialized/compressed model without losing identity."

Do NOT claim that a 1MB file alone can replace a full general LLM. That is not proven and is
information-theoretically extremely hard because vocabulary/embeddings/LM-head alone are huge.

================================================================
MODEL AND HARDWARE CONTEXT
================================================================

Primary model used:
- Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct
  - 24 layers
  - hidden dim = 896
  - vocab size = 151936

Later scaled to:
- Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct
  - 28 layers
  - hidden dim = 1536

Hardware used:
- Initially CPU-only validation on 0.5B
- Later T4 GPU for 0.5B/1.5B fp16 experiments

Key prompts used for calibration/testing:
- "The capital of France is"
- "The capital of Japan is"
- "The capital of Mexico is"
- "The chemical symbol for silver is"
- plus expanded geography/chemistry/Python/common-fact prompts.

================================================================
WHAT WAS ORIGINALLY TRIED AND WHY IT FAILED
================================================================

PHASE 1: DELTA DNA / FLAT RANK-16 SVD

Idea:
For every transformer layer, compute:
    delta = layer_output - layer_input

Collect deltas from calibration prompts.
Run SVD on deltas.
Keep top Rank-16 basis vectors.
During generation, replace each layer delta with its Rank-16 reconstruction.

Observed:
- Internal shift direction was preserved very well.
- Logit Lens showed approximately 96.4% average internal shift preservation.
- Late layers were even better, around 99%.
- BUT final autoregressive generation collapsed to around 23% useful accuracy.
- Identity collapse occurred:
    Japan vs Mexico original hidden distance = 0.1119
    After flat Rank-16 DNA distance = 0.0001
    Ratio = 0.001
- Japan, Mexico, France became almost indistinguishable in compressed space.

Conclusion:
Variance preservation is not behavior preservation.
Flat SVD captures high-variance grammar/template directions and destroys low-variance identity directions.

================================================================
PROBLEM 1 SOLVED: IDENTITY COLLAPSE
================================================================

Problem:
Flat Rank-16 SVD compresses the big common template:
    "The capital of ___ is"
but deletes the tiny entity-specific signal:
    Japan vs Mexico vs France.

Solution discovered:
SPIRAL ZOOM DNA / HIERARCHICAL RESIDUAL SVD

Instead of taking one flat Rank-16 basis, take residual zoom levels:

Algorithm:
1. Start with delta matrix from calibration prompts.
2. Subtract mean mu.
3. Level 1:
   - SVD residual
   - take top r1 vectors, e.g. Rank 4 or Rank 16
   - project residual onto those vectors
   - subtract projection
4. Level 2:
   - SVD the remaining residual
   - take top r2 vectors
   - project and subtract
5. Level 3:
   - SVD the next remaining residual
   - take top r3 vectors
   - project and subtract
6. Reconstruction:
   delta_reconstructed = mu + projection_level1 + projection_level2 + projection_level3

Why this works:
Level 1 captures broad grammar/template.
Level 2 captures contextual/domain structure.
Level 3 captures small residual identity directions that flat SVD would discard.

Proof/result:
Using Spiral Zoom with 3 levels x Rank 4:
- Japan-Mexico identity preservation ratio improved from about 0.004 to 0.803
- Japan-France improved to about 0.762
- Mexico-France improved to about 0.645
- Average identity preservation ratio became about 0.737

This was the first major breakthrough.
It proved that LLM knowledge is hierarchical/fractal in activation space, not flat.

================================================================
PROBLEM 2 PARTIALLY SOLVED: AUTOREGRESSIVE DRIFT / BUTTERFLY EFFECT
================================================================

Problem:
Even if Step 0 is good, once the model generates its own token, the context changes.
The compressed DNA was calibrated on static prompts, not on model-generated trajectories.
Small reconstruction errors compound through 24/28 nonlinear layers.

Symptoms:
- Original model:
    "The capital of Japan is Tokyo..."
- Flat DNA:
    gibberish, blanks, repetition, wrong entities.
- Spiral DNA generation:
    sometimes got "Tokyo" but later tokens drifted.

Solutions tried:

A. LOOKAHEAD / MANIFOLD-CONSTRAINED DECODING
For each candidate next token, temporarily append it, run forward pass, measure projection error
against the Spiral DNA manifold. Score candidates by:
    score = log(probability) - alpha * projection_error
This reduced obvious gibberish and helped produce:
    "The capital of Japan is located Tokyo..."
    "The capital of Mexico is located Mexico..."
But it caused repetition because safe structural tokens had lowest error.

B. REPETITION BRAKES
Added penalty for recently used tokens:
    score -= repetition_penalty if token in recent window
This reduced loops but still left artifacts.

C. SOFT DNA BLENDING
Instead of fully replacing delta with DNA delta, blend:
    delta_used = soft * original_delta + (1 - soft) * dna_delta
where soft=1 means original model, soft=0 means full DNA.

Results on Qwen2.5-1.5B with Spiral rank 96:
- Hard DNA (soft=0) still unstable.
- Soft 0.25/0.50/0.75 improved coherence.
- Soft 0.50 produced meaningful outputs:
    "The capital of Japan is Tokyo, which has a population..."
    "The capital of Mexico is a city named Mexico City..."
    "The chemical symbol for silver is Ag, which has a mass..."

D. LoRA DISTILLATION WITH DNA ON
This was the strongest practical fix.

Architecture:
Teacher:
    original model, DNA OFF, LoRA OFF
Student:
    same base model + tiny LoRA adapter + Spiral DNA ON at Soft 0.50

Training loss:
    L = top-K KL divergence(student logits, teacher logits)
        + lambda * normalized hidden-state MSE
        + optional auxiliary coefficient/delta loss

Result:
After short T4 distillation:
- Soft DNA 0.50 became much more coherent.
- Entity hits improved:
    Japan -> Tokyo
    Silver -> Ag
    Mexico-related output coherent
- Repetition dropped to 0.000 in tested prompts.
- Gibberish became low.
- Hard DNA still weaker, but Soft DNA + LoRA became a credible product lane.

Conclusion:
Post-hoc mathematical correction alone is not enough for stable autoregressive generation.
A tiny learned adapter trained while DNA is active can absorb nonlinear drift.

================================================================
PROBLEM 3 MAPPED: NON-LINEAR CLIFFS / KNOWLEDGE VOIDS
================================================================

Problem:
Compression errors push hidden states into regions where the model has no stable knowledge.

Experiment:
Input-Gate Liquid / Contrastive Interpolation.
Instead of injecting probes inside layers, feed interpolated embeddings directly through input gate.

Example:
Embedding mix between France and Japan:
    alpha = 0.0 to 1.0
    emb_mix = (1-alpha)*France_embedding + alpha*Japan_embedding

Observation:
- Near pure France: model predicts Paris.
- Near pure Japan: model predicts located/Tokyo-related structure.
- Around 50/50: probability collapses and model outputs blanks/gibberish.

This proved existence of a nonlinear cliff/void between concept manifolds.

Tried to bridge with SLERP:
Spherical linear interpolation instead of linear interpolation.
Result:
SLERP did not remove the void. Both LERP and SLERP fell into the same valley around 50/50.

Conclusion:
The problem is not just straight-line interpolation geometry.
The model has separated knowledge islands with unstable transition regions.

Tried Topological Mold / Static Snap:
Freeze hidden states of calibration concepts and snap current state toward nearest island.
Result:
Static snapping pulled model backward toward prompt-end states and worsened generation.

Lesson:
Anchors must be trajectory-aware, not static prompt-end anchors.

================================================================
PROBLEM 4 ADDRESSED: STATIC DNA vs INPUT-CONDITIONED DYNAMIC DNA
================================================================

Problem:
Static Spiral DNA bases are fixed after calibration.
But generation context changes every token.
A fixed basis can become out-of-distribution.

Solution tried:
Dynamic DNA / HyperNetwork coefficient generator.

Architecture:
Store:
- per-layer mean mu[l]
- per-layer Spiral basis V[l] of shape (total_rank, hidden_dim)
- per-layer coefficient scale scale[l]

Tiny HyperNet:
Input:
    current hidden state x at layer l
    layer id l
Output:
    alpha coefficients of shape total_rank

Generated DNA delta:
    delta_dna = mu[l] + (alpha * scale[l]) @ V[l]

Important correction-only blend:
Do NOT fully replace original delta.
Use:
    delta_used = delta_original + gamma * (delta_dna - delta_original)

Training:
Phase 1:
Offline coefficient pretraining:
    teach HyperNet to predict alpha such that delta_dna approximates teacher delta.

Phase 2:
Behavior fine-tuning:
    run base model with DNA correction ON
    match teacher logits and teacher final hidden states
    keep gamma small, e.g. 0.03 to 0.15

Results:
On Qwen2.5-0.5B with ~0.78MB total DNA:
- HyperNet did become input-conditioned.
- Different prompts produced different outputs.
- Offline average cosine improved to about 0.55.
- Some entity steering appeared:
    Japan at gamma=0.10 produced "Tokyo" in continuation.
    Silver at gamma=0.02 preserved original-like output.
- But full stable generation was still weak.
- Higher gamma produced multilingual artifacts/gibberish.

Conclusion:
Dynamic DNA is a valid mechanism.
Input-conditioned parameter generation works.
But 1MB capacity is too small for full stable behavioral replacement on even 0.5B/1.5B models.
It can serve as a correction module, not yet as a standalone brain.

================================================================
PROBLEM 5 TESTED AND KILLED: WEIGHT-SPACE FOURIER DNA
================================================================

Old dream:
Maybe weights themselves are smooth functions W[i,j] = f(i,j), so a tiny Fourier DNA can generate
large weight matrices from coordinates.

Test A:
Train Fourier DNA to reconstruct a real Qwen layer q_proj weight matrix:
    shape 896x896
    Fourier DNA params ~50k
Comparison:
    SVD Rank-16 params ~28k, cosine ~0.865
    SVD Rank-32 params ~57k, cosine ~0.917
    Fourier DNA cosine ~0.241
Result:
FAIL. Real weights are not smooth coordinate functions.

Test B:
Train Fourier DNA on all 24 q_proj matrices stacked:
    shape (24,896,896)
Result:
cosine near 0.
No smooth cross-layer weight evolution.

Test C:
Fourier HyperNet for coefficient prediction:
Also failed to provide meaningful advantage in this setup.

Conclusion:
Weight-space compression via Fourier coordinate functions is a dead end for LLM behavioral compression.
Knowledge is not usefully stored as smooth weight-coordinate functions.
The productive space is activation/residual-stream geometry.

================================================================
VALIDATED FINAL ARCHITECTURE
================================================================

The current best proven architecture is:

BASE MODEL
    +
SPIRAL ZOOM ACTIVATION DNA
    +
SOFT DNA BLENDING / CORRECTION
    +
TINY LoRA DISTILLATION
    +
OPTIONAL DYNAMIC HYPERNET COEFFICIENT GENERATOR

Detailed flow:

1. Calibration:
   Run base model on diverse prompts.
   For each layer l, capture:
       input hidden state x
       output hidden state y
       delta = y - x

2. Build Spiral basis:
   For each layer:
       compute mean mu[l]
       residual SVD over multiple zoom levels
       store basis vectors V[l]
       store coefficient scales if using dynamic DNA

3. Generation with Soft DNA:
   At each layer:
       compute original delta from base model
       reconstruct DNA delta using Spiral basis
       blend:
           delta_used = soft * original_delta + (1-soft) * dna_delta
       or correction-only:
           delta_used = original_delta + gamma * (dna_delta - original_delta)

4. LoRA distillation:
   Train only LoRA adapter while DNA is active.
   Teacher = original model without DNA.
   Student = model with DNA + LoRA.
   Loss:
       top-K KL on logits
       normalized hidden-state MSE
       optional auxiliary delta loss

5. Optional Dynamic DNA:
   HyperNet reads current hidden state and layer id.
   Predicts coefficients alpha.
   Generates delta_dna = mu + (alpha*scale) @ V.
   Apply as small correction, not full replacement.

================================================================
WHAT IS PROVEN
================================================================

PROVEN 1:
LLM behavioral knowledge is much more compressible in activation/residual space than in raw weights.

PROVEN 2:
Flat low-rank SVD causes identity collapse because it prioritizes high-variance template directions.

PROVEN 3:
Spiral Zoom / hierarchical residual SVD preserves close-entity identity much better.
Japan-Mexico ratio moved from ~0.001/0.004 to ~0.803 in offline identity tests.

PROVEN 4:
Internal shift direction can be preserved ~96% while final generation still fails.
Therefore behavior metrics must be measured at logits/generation level, not only cosine/variance.

PROVEN 5:
Autoregressive drift is a real nonlinear compounding problem.
Static post-hoc rescue matrices/MLPs are insufficient.

PROVEN 6:
Soft DNA blending is much more stable than hard DNA replacement.

PROVEN 7:
Tiny LoRA distillation with DNA active can stabilize generation and recover factual entities like:
    Tokyo
    Mexico City
    Ag

PROVEN 8:
Dynamic DNA can generate input-conditioned coefficients and alter behavior differently per prompt.
This validates the "input ke hisaab se params generate" idea as a mechanism.

PROVEN 9:
1MB dynamic DNA is possible as an artifact size, but not yet sufficient for full stable standalone generation.

PROVEN 10:
Fourier weight-coordinate generation is not a viable path for real LLM weight compression.

================================================================
WHAT IS NOT PROVEN / STILL OPEN
================================================================

NOT PROVEN 1:
A ~1MB .dna file alone can replace a 0.5B/1.5B/3B LLM for general text generation.

NOT PROVEN 2:
Hard DNA (soft=0) can produce stable long coherent generation without base model support.

NOT PROVEN 3:
Dynamic DNA at 1MB can fully preserve thinking style, grammar, facts, and reasoning without artifacts.

NOT PROVEN 4:
The method scales cleanly to 7B/8B models without larger DNA or stronger distillation.

OPEN ISSUE 1:
Nonlinear cliffs/voids between close concepts still exist.

OPEN ISSUE 2:
Trajectory-aware dynamic anchoring is needed.
Static anchors can pull model backward.

OPEN ISSUE 3:
Rollout / scheduled sampling training is likely required for true autoregressive stability.

OPEN ISSUE 4:
Minimum viable DNA size must be measured:
    1MB, 2MB, 4MB, 8MB, 16MB, 32MB
against behavioral quality.

================================================================
KEY METRICS TO USE IN FUTURE WORK
================================================================

Do not rely only on reconstruction MSE or cosine.
Use behavioral metrics:

1. entity_hit_continuation:
   Check whether expected entity appears in generated continuation, not prompt.
   Example:
       prompt: "The capital of Japan is"
       expected: "Tokyo"
       check only text after prompt.

2. prefix_match:
   Number of generated tokens matching original model's generated prefix.

3. repetition_score:
   Fraction of repeated adjacent words/tokens.

4. gibberish_score:
   Ratio of punctuation/symbols/invalid characters to letters.

5. relative reconstruction error:
   ||delta_true - delta_dna|| / ||delta_true||

6. top-K KL divergence:
   Student DNA-on logits vs teacher original logits.

7. hidden-state normalized MSE:
   Student final hidden state vs teacher final hidden state.

8. identity preservation ratio:
   DNA pairwise distance / original pairwise distance for concept pairs.
   Good if close to 1.
   Bad if near 0.

================================================================
WHAT THE NEXT AI SHOULD DO
================================================================

Your job is NOT to rediscover failed paths.
Do not spend time on:
- Fourier DNA for raw weights
- static post-hoc rescue matrix
- pure hard DNA replacement
- claiming 1MB standalone general LLM

Your job is to build the proven direction into a product/research system.

Priority 1: Package the working DNA Cartridge.
Implement a clean loader/runtime that can:
- load base Qwen model
- load Spiral DNA artifact (.npz or .dna)
- load optional LoRA adapter
- inject layer hooks
- run generation with Soft DNA
- report behavioral metrics

Priority 2: Expand benchmark.
Test at least 50 prompts across:
- capitals
- chemistry
- Python
- common facts
- close entity pairs
- unseen entities
Measure:
- entity_hit_continuation
- prefix match
- repetition
- gibberish
- KL to teacher
- identity preservation ratio

Priority 3: Find minimum viable DNA size.
Run size sweep:
- Rank total 16, 32, 64, 96, 128, 192
- DNA size approx 1MB, 2MB, 4MB, 8MB, 16MB
Plot behavior quality vs DNA size.
This becomes the startup's core empirical curve.

Priority 4: Improve Dynamic DNA safely.
Use Dynamic HyperNet only as low-gamma correction.
Train with:
- offline coefficient pretraining
- behavior fine-tuning
- rollout/scheduled sampling
- normalized losses
- alpha clipping
- trajectory anchors from generated sequences, not only prompt-end states.

Priority 5: Product positioning.
Correct claim:
"We compress LLM task behavior into a small activation-DNA cartridge plus micro adapter.
Instead of downloading a new multi-GB specialized model, load a few-MB behavior cartridge into
an existing base LLM."

Incorrect claim:
"One 1MB file replaces the entire LLM."

================================================================
DESIGN PRINCIPLES LEARNED
================================================================

1. Knowledge lives in activation geometry, not raw weight smoothness.
2. High variance != important for behavior.
3. Low-variance directions can carry critical identity.
4. Compression must be hierarchical, not flat.
5. Generation stability requires behavior-level training, not just delta reconstruction.
6. Soft blending beats hard replacement.
7. Tiny learned adapters can absorb nonlinear drift.
8. Dynamic input-conditioned coefficients are promising but need larger capacity and rollout training.
9. Nonlinear voids between concepts are real and must be detected, not ignored.
10. A DNA cartridge is currently a steering module, not a full model replacement.

================================================================
MINIMAL REPRODUCTION EXPERIMENT
================================================================

If you need to validate quickly on T4:

Model:
Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct or Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct

Steps:
1. Capture layer deltas on calibration prompts.
2. Build Spiral Zoom basis per layer.
3. Run generation with:
   - original model
   - hard DNA
   - Soft DNA 0.25/0.50/0.75
4. Add tiny LoRA distillation with DNA ON.
5. Evaluate:
   - "The capital of Japan is" -> expect Tokyo
   - "The capital of Mexico is" -> expect Mexico City/Mexico
   - "The chemical symbol for silver is" -> expect Ag
6. Save DNA artifact and adapter.

Expected proven result:
Soft DNA + LoRA should produce substantially more coherent entity-preserving output than flat/hard DNA.

Expected unresolved result:
Hard DNA and 1MB dynamic DNA may still show artifacts.

================================================================
FINAL ONE-LINE SUMMARY
================================================================

Reverse DNA is not a magic 1MB file that replaces an LLM; it is a proven activation-space
behavioral cartridge system: Spiral Zoom preserves identity, Soft DNA stabilizes geometry,
tiny LoRA absorbs nonlinear drift, and Dynamic HyperNet adds input-conditioned parameter generation.# ============================================================
# 🧪 STEP 1 MATH PROOF: FLAT RANK-12 vs SPIRAL 4+4+4
# + HELD-OUT IDENTITY TEST + EFFECTIVE RANK CHECK
# CPU-friendly, Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct
# ============================================================
import os
import time
import gc
import itertools
import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer

try:
    torch.set_num_threads(max(1, (os.cpu_count() or 1) - 1))
except Exception:
    pass

t0 = time.time()
print("=" * 88)
print("🧪 STEP 1 MATH PROOF: FLAT RANK-12 vs SPIRAL 4+4+4")
print("=" * 88)

MODEL = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct"

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 1. Load model
# ------------------------------------------------------------
try:
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
        MODEL,
        dtype=torch.float32,
        low_cpu_mem_usage=True
    )
except TypeError:
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
        MODEL,
        torch_dtype=torch.float32,
        low_cpu_mem_usage=True
    )

tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL)
tok.padding_side = "left"
if tok.pad_token is None:
    tok.pad_token = tok.eos_token

model.eval()

NL = model.config.num_hidden_layers
D = model.config.hidden_size

print(f"✅ Model loaded | layers={NL} | hidden={D}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 2. Config
# ------------------------------------------------------------
TOTAL_RANK = 12
SPIRAL_LEVELS = 3
RANK_PER_LEVEL = 4

# Calibration concepts
CAL_NAMES = ["France", "Japan", "Mexico", "Silver"]

# Held-out concepts: ye calibration mein NAHI hone chahiye
HELDOUT_NAMES = ["Brazil", "Germany", "India", "Gold"]

PROMPTS = {
    "France": "The capital of France is",
    "Japan": "The capital of Japan is",
    "Mexico": "The capital of Mexico is",
    "Silver": "The chemical symbol for silver is",

    "Brazil": "The capital of Brazil is",
    "Germany": "The capital of Germany is",
    "India": "The capital of India is",
    "Gold": "The chemical symbol for gold is",
}

CAL_PROMPTS = [PROMPTS[n] for n in CAL_NAMES]

print(f"🧠 Calibration prompts: {CAL_NAMES}")
print(f"🧪 Held-out prompts : {HELDOUT_NAMES}")
print(f"⚙️ Flat rank={TOTAL_RANK}, Spiral={SPIRAL_LEVELS}x{RANK_PER_LEVEL}={SPIRAL_LEVELS*RANK_PER_LEVEL}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 3. Capture calibration deltas
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n📐 Capturing calibration deltas...")

cal_inp = tok(
    CAL_PROMPTS,
    return_tensors="pt",
    padding=True,
    truncation=True,
    max_length=24
)
cal_mask = cal_inp["attention_mask"].bool()

boxes = {l: {} for l in range(NL)}

def make_capture_hook(l_idx):
    def hook(m, i, o):
        boxes[l_idx]["in"] = i[0].detach().float()
        boxes[l_idx]["out"] = o.detach().float()
        return o
    return hook

capture_hooks = [
    model.model.layers[l].register_forward_hook(make_capture_hook(l))
    for l in range(NL)
]

with torch.no_grad():
    model(**cal_inp)

for h in capture_hooks:
    h.remove()

n_valid_tokens = int(cal_mask.sum().item())
print(f"✅ Calibration valid tokens n={n_valid_tokens}")

cal_flat = {}
for l in range(NL):
    delta = boxes[l]["out"] - boxes[l]["in"]      # (B, L, D)
    flat = delta[cal_mask]                        # (n_valid_tokens, D)
    cal_flat[l] = flat

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 4. Build Flat Rank-12 and Spiral 4+4+4 bases
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n🧠 Building bases...")

flat_bases = {}
spiral_bases = {}
eff_ranks = {}
spiral_used_ranks = {}

for l in range(NL):
    X = cal_flat[l].double().numpy()              # (n, D)
    if X.shape[0] == 0:
        flat_bases[l] = (torch.zeros((0, D), dtype=torch.float32), torch.zeros(D, dtype=torch.float32))
        spiral_bases[l] = ([], torch.zeros(D, dtype=torch.float32))
        eff_ranks[l] = 0
        spiral_used_ranks[l] = 0
        continue

    mu = X.mean(axis=0)
    Xc = X - mu

    # Full SVD for numerical rank estimate
    U, S, Vh = np.linalg.svd(Xc, full_matrices=False)

    if S.size > 0 and S[0] > 0:
        tol = max(Xc.shape) * float(S[0]) * 1e-12
        num_rank = int(np.sum(S > tol))
    else:
        num_rank = 0

    eff = min(TOTAL_RANK, num_rank, D)
    eff_ranks[l] = eff

    # Flat basis
    Vk_flat = torch.tensor(Vh[:eff], dtype=torch.float32)
    mu_t = torch.tensor(mu, dtype=torch.float32)
    flat_bases[l] = (Vk_flat, mu_t)

    # Spiral basis: sequential residual SVD
    residual = Xc.copy()
    levels = []
    used = 0

    for z in range(SPIRAL_LEVELS):
        if residual.size == 0:
            break

        norm_res = float(np.linalg.norm(residual))
        if norm_res < 1e-10:
            break

        U2, S2, Vh2 = np.linalg.svd(residual, full_matrices=False)
        take = min(RANK_PER_LEVEL, Vh2.shape[0])
        if take == 0:
            break

        Vz = Vh2[:take]
        levels.append(torch.tensor(Vz, dtype=torch.float32))
        used += take

        proj = residual @ Vz.T @ Vz
        residual = residual - proj

    spiral_bases[l] = (levels, mu_t)
    spiral_used_ranks[l] = used

min_eff = int(np.min(list(eff_ranks.values()))) if eff_ranks else 0
max_eff = int(np.max(list(eff_ranks.values()))) if eff_ranks else 0
min_spiral_used = int(np.min(list(spiral_used_ranks.values()))) if spiral_used_ranks else 0
max_spiral_used = int(np.max(list(spiral_used_ranks.values()))) if spiral_used_ranks else 0

print("✅ Bases built")
print(f"   Effective flat rank per layer: min={min_eff}, max={max_eff}, requested={TOTAL_RANK}")
print(f"   Spiral used rank per layer : min={min_spiral_used}, max={max_spiral_used}, requested={SPIRAL_LEVELS*RANK_PER_LEVEL}")
print(f"   Max possible centered rank from n tokens: {max(0, n_valid_tokens - 1)}")

if min_eff < TOTAL_RANK:
    print("⚠️ WARNING: Kuch layers mein effective rank requested rank se kam hai.")
    print("   Iska matlab compression fully active nahi ho paya us layer mein.")

if min_spiral_used < SPIRAL_LEVELS * RANK_PER_LEVEL:
    print("⚠️ WARNING: Spiral ne requested total rank se kam vectors use kiye.")
    print("   Comparison thoda unfair ho sakta hai; logs check karo.")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 5. DNA hooks
# ------------------------------------------------------------
STATE = {"mode": "off"}  # off | flat | spiral

def make_dna_hook(l_idx):
    def hook(m, i, o):
        mode = STATE["mode"]
        if mode == "off":
            return o

        x = i[0].float()
        delta = o.float() - x
        fd = delta.reshape(-1, D)

        if mode == "flat":
            Vk, mu = flat_bases[l_idx]
            if Vk.shape[0] == 0:
                return o
            centered = fd - mu
            proj = centered @ Vk.T @ Vk
            recon = mu + proj

        elif mode == "spiral":
            levels, mu = spiral_bases[l_idx]
            if not levels:
                return o

            current = fd - mu
            recon = torch.zeros_like(current) + mu

            for Vk in levels:
                proj = current @ Vk.T @ Vk
                recon = recon + proj
                current = current - proj

        else:
            return o

        return (x + recon.reshape(o.shape)).to(o.dtype)
    return hook

dna_hooks = [
    model.model.layers[l].register_forward_hook(make_dna_hook(l))
    for l in range(NL)
]

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 6. Get hidden-state flow vector for a prompt
# ------------------------------------------------------------
def get_flow_vector(prompt, mode):
    ids = tok(prompt, return_tensors="pt").input_ids

    STATE["mode"] = mode
    with torch.no_grad():
        out = model(ids, output_hidden_states=True)

    # hidden_states[0] = embedding
    # hidden_states[l+1] = output of layer l
    H = torch.stack(
        [out.hidden_states[l + 1][0, -1, :].float() for l in range(NL)],
        dim=0
    )  # (NL, D)

    STATE["mode"] = "off"

    v = H.reshape(-1)
    v = F.normalize(v, dim=0)
    return v

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 7. Compute vectors for all prompts under original / flat / spiral
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n Computing identity vectors for all prompts...")

orig_vec = {}
flat_vec = {}
spiral_vec = {}

for name, prompt in PROMPTS.items():
    orig_vec[name] = get_flow_vector(prompt, "off")
    flat_vec[name] = get_flow_vector(prompt, "flat")
    spiral_vec[name] = get_flow_vector(prompt, "spiral")

print(f"✅ Vectors ready ({time.time()-t0:.1f}s)")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 8. Distance and ratio
# ------------------------------------------------------------
def cos_dist(a, b):
    return float(1.0 - torch.dot(a, b).clamp(-1.0, 1.0).item())

rows = []

for scope, names in [
    ("IN_SAMPLE", CAL_NAMES),
    ("HELD_OUT", HELDOUT_NAMES),
]:
    for a, b in itertools.combinations(names, 2):
        od = cos_dist(orig_vec[a], orig_vec[b])

        fd = cos_dist(flat_vec[a], flat_vec[b])
        sd = cos_dist(spiral_vec[a], spiral_vec[b])

        fr = fd / od if od > 1e-6 else 1.0
        sr = sd / od if od > 1e-6 else 1.0

        rows.append({
            "scope": scope,
            "pair": f"{a}-{b}",
            "orig_dist": od,
            "flat_dist": fd,
            "flat_ratio": fr,
            "spiral_dist": sd,
            "spiral_ratio": sr,
        })

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 9. Print table
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n" + "=" * 88)
print("📊 IDENTITY PRESERVATION TABLE")
print("   Ratio = DNA distance / Original distance")
print("   1.0 = identity preserved, 0.0 = identity collapsed")
print("=" * 88)

print(
    f"{'Scope':<10}{'Pair':<18}{'OrigDist':<10}"
    f"{'FlatDist':<10}{'FlatRatio':<11}{'SpiralDist':<11}{'SpiralRatio'}"
)
print("-" * 88)

for r in rows:
    print(
        f"{r['scope']:<10}{r['pair']:<18}{r['orig_dist']:<10.4f}"
        f"{r['flat_dist']:<10.4f}{r['flat_ratio']:<11.3f}"
        f"{r['spiral_dist']:<11.4f}{r['spiral_ratio']:.3f}"
    )

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 10. Averages
# ------------------------------------------------------------
def avg(xs):
    return float(np.mean(xs)) if xs else 0.0

in_sample = [r for r in rows if r["scope"] == "IN_SAMPLE"]
held_out = [r for r in rows if r["scope"] == "HELD_OUT"]

avg_in_flat = avg([r["flat_ratio"] for r in in_sample])
avg_in_spiral = avg([r["spiral_ratio"] for r in in_sample])

avg_held_flat = avg([r["flat_ratio"] for r in held_out])
avg_held_spiral = avg([r["spiral_ratio"] for r in held_out])

print("\n" + "=" * 88)
print("📈 AVERAGE IDENTITY RATIOS")
print("=" * 88)
print(f"{'Scope':<12}{'Flat Rank-12':<18}{'Spiral 4+4+4':<18}{'Diff (Spiral-Flat)'}")
print("-" * 88)
print(f"{'IN_SAMPLE':<12}{avg_in_flat:<18.3f}{avg_in_spiral:<18.3f}{avg_in_spiral - avg_in_flat:+.3f}")
print(f"{'HELD_OUT':<12}{avg_held_flat:<18.3f}{avg_held_spiral:<18.3f}{avg_held_spiral - avg_held_flat:+.3f}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 11. Verdict
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n" + "=" * 88)
print("🏆 STEP 1 VERDICT")
print("=" * 88)

diff_held = avg_held_spiral - avg_held_flat

print(f"Held-out Flat average   : {avg_held_flat:.3f}")
print(f"Held-out Spiral average : {avg_held_spiral:.3f}")
print(f"Difference              : {diff_held:+.3f}")
print()

if min_eff < TOTAL_RANK or min_spiral_used < SPIRAL_LEVELS * RANK_PER_LEVEL:
    print("⚠️ RANK WARNING:")
    print("   Effective rank / spiral used rank requested se kam ho sakta hai.")
    print("   Isliye result ko 'proof' se pehle 'diagnostic' samjho.")
    print()

if avg_held_spiral < 0.20 and avg_held_flat < 0.20:
    print("🔴 BOTH COLLAPSED ON HELD-OUT ENTITIES")
    print("   Flat aur Spiral dono held-out identity preserve nahi kar paye.")
    print("   Matlab hierarchical residual SVD akela solution nahi hai.")
    print("   Next: contrastive/held-out-aware basis ya LoRA/distillation route.")

elif abs(diff_held) < 0.02:
    print("⚖️ SPIRAL ≈ FLAT AT EQUAL RANK")
    print("   Spiral 4+4+4 aur Flat Rank-12 ka held-out result almost same hai.")
    print("   Iska matlab: 'Spiral Zoom mathematically flat SVD se better hai' — ye claim abhi proven nahi.")
    print("   Next: ya toh different rank allocation chahiye, ya held-out contrastive directions chahiye.")

elif diff_held > 0.05:
    print("✅ SPIRAL BETTER THAN FLAT ON HELD-OUT")
    print("   Spiral 4+4+4 ne held-out identity ratio Flat Rank-12 se behtar di.")
    print("   Ye promising hai, lekin abhi bhi generation proof baaki hai.")
    print("   Next: LoRA ablation + rollout training.")

else:
    print("🔻 SPIRAL WORSE THAN FLAT ON HELD-OUT")
    print("   Spiral ne flat se kharab held-out identity di.")
    print("   Hierarchical residual SVD is setting mein help nahi kar raha.")
    print("   Next: approach revise karo.")

print("\n💡 NOTE:")
print("   Ye sirf offline hidden-state geometry test hai.")
print("   Ye generation accuracy nahi hai.")
print("   Agar ye pass hota hai, tabhi Step 2 (LoRA ablation) ka matlab banta hai.")

print(f"\n⏱️ Total time: {time.time()-t0:.1f}s")

# cleanup
for h in dna_hooks:
    h.remove()

del model
gc.collect() ========================================================================================

# 🧬 PD-1: KNOWLEDGE WAVE DNA — 7 TESTS / MATH PROOFS IN ONE CELL
# Goal: Prove that teacher knowledge can be represented as waves/functions,
#       and input can generate temporary params from a tiny DNA artifact.
# Model: Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct
# Hardware: CPU-friendly
# ============================================================

import os
import time
import gc
import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F
from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer

try:
    torch.set_num_threads(max(1, (os.cpu_count() or 1) - 1))
except Exception:
    pass

t0 = time.time()

print("=" * 96)
print("🧬 PD-1: KNOWLEDGE WAVE DNA — 7 TESTS / MATH PROOFS IN ONE CELL")
print("=" * 96)

MODEL = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct"

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 1. Load teacher model
# ------------------------------------------------------------
try:
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
        MODEL,
        dtype=torch.float32,
        low_cpu_mem_usage=True
    )
except TypeError:
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
        MODEL,
        torch_dtype=torch.float32,
        low_cpu_mem_usage=True
    )

tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL)
tok.padding_side = "left"
if tok.pad_token is None:
    tok.pad_token = tok.eos_token

model.eval()

NL = model.config.num_hidden_layers
D = model.config.hidden_size

print(f"✅ Teacher loaded | layers={NL} | hidden={D}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 2. Concepts and prompts
# ------------------------------------------------------------
COUNTRIES = {
    "France", "Japan", "Mexico", "Brazil", "Germany",
    "India", "Canada", "Australia", "Argentina"
}

CAL_CONCEPTS = [
    "France", "Japan", "Mexico", "Silver", "Gold"
]

HELD_CONCEPTS = [
    "Brazil", "Germany", "India", "Platinum", "Copper"
]

ALL_CONCEPTS = CAL_CONCEPTS + HELD_CONCEPTS
concept_to_id = {c: i for i, c in enumerate(ALL_CONCEPTS)}
C_all = len(ALL_CONCEPTS)

def make_prompts(concept):
    if concept in COUNTRIES:
        cal = [
            f"The capital of {concept} is",
            f"{concept}'s capital is",
            f"Please name the capital of {concept}:"
        ]
        held = f"What is the capital of {concept}?"
    else:
        cal = [
            f"The chemical symbol for {concept} is",
            f"{concept} has the chemical symbol",
            f"Please give the chemical symbol for {concept}:"
        ]
        held = f"What is the chemical symbol for {concept}?"
    return cal, held

cal_prompts = []
cal_labels = []

held_phrase_prompts = []
held_phrase_labels = []

held_concept_prompts = []
held_concept_labels = []

for c in CAL_CONCEPTS:
    cps, hp = make_prompts(c)
    cal_prompts.extend(cps)
    cal_labels.extend([c] * len(cps))

    held_phrase_prompts.append(hp)
    held_phrase_labels.append(c)

for c in HELD_CONCEPTS:
    cps, _ = make_prompts(c)
    held_concept_prompts.extend(cps)
    held_concept_labels.extend([c] * len(cps))

print(f"🧠 Calibration prompts: {len(cal_prompts)}")
print(f"🧪 Held-out phrase prompts: {len(held_phrase_prompts)}")
print(f"🧪 Held-out concept prompts: {len(held_concept_prompts)}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 3. Helper: capture layer-wise response vector + mean embedding
# ------------------------------------------------------------
embed_layer = model.get_input_embeddings()

def get_response_and_embedding(prompts, batch_size=4):
    all_resp = []
    all_emb = []

    for start in range(0, len(prompts), batch_size):
        batch = prompts[start:start + batch_size]

        inp = tok(
            batch,
            return_tensors="pt",
            padding=True,
            truncation=True,
            max_length=32
        )
        mask = inp["attention_mask"].bool()

        with torch.inference_mode():
            out = model(**inp, output_hidden_states=True)
            emb = embed_layer(inp["input_ids"]).float()

        # left padding => real last token is final column
        H = torch.stack(
            [out.hidden_states[l + 1][:, -1, :].float() for l in range(NL)],
            dim=1
        )  # (B, NL, D)

        resp = H.reshape(H.shape[0], -1)  # (B, NL*D)

        mean_emb = (emb * mask.unsqueeze(-1)).sum(dim=1)
        mean_emb = mean_emb / mask.sum(dim=1, keepdim=True).clamp(min=1)

        all_resp.append(resp.cpu())
        all_emb.append(mean_emb.cpu())

    return torch.cat(all_resp, dim=0), torch.cat(all_emb, dim=0)

print("\n📐 Capturing teacher response waves...")

Xcal_resp, Xcal_emb = get_response_and_embedding(cal_prompts)
Xhold_phrase_resp, Xhold_phrase_emb = get_response_and_embedding(held_phrase_prompts)
Xheld_concept_resp, Xheld_concept_emb = get_response_and_embedding(held_concept_prompts)

M = Xcal_resp.shape[1]
print(f"✅ Response wave dimension: layers*hidden = {NL}*{D} = {M}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 4. Center calibration response matrix
# ------------------------------------------------------------
mu = Xcal_resp.mean(dim=0)
Xc = Xcal_resp - mu

print("\n🧮 Full SVD of calibration response waves...")
with torch.inference_mode():
    U, S, Vh = torch.linalg.svd(Xc.double(), full_matrices=False)

Vh_float = Vh.float()

if S.numel() > 0 and float(S[0]) > 0:
    tol = max(Xc.shape) * float(S[0]) * 1e-12
    eff_rank = int((S > tol).sum().item())
else:
    eff_rank = 0

print(f"✅ Numerical effective rank of calibration wave matrix: {eff_rank}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 5. Utility: cosine rows
# ------------------------------------------------------------
def cos_rows(A, B):
    A = A.float()
    B = B.float()
    num = (A * B).sum(dim=1)
    den = A.norm(dim=1) * B.norm(dim=1)
    cos = torch.where(den > 1e-8, num / den.clamp(min=1e-8), torch.ones_like(num))
    return float(cos.mean().item()), cos

def avg_cos_per_group(mat, labels, group_names):
    vals = []
    for g in group_names:
        idx = [i for i, x in enumerate(labels) if x == g]
        if not idx:
            continue
        sub = mat[idx]
        center = sub.mean(dim=0, keepdim=True)
        c, _ = cos_rows(sub - center, sub - center)
        vals.append(c)
    return float(np.mean(vals)) if vals else 0.0

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# TEST 1 + 2: Wave basis rank sweep
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n" + "=" * 96)
print("🧪 TEST 1 + 2: KNOWLEDGE WAVE BASIS RANK SWEEP")
print("=" * 96)

RANKS = [r for r in [4, 8, 12, 16] if r <= eff_rank]
if not RANKS:
    RANKS = [max(1, eff_rank)]

print(f"{'Rank':<7}{'CalWaveCos':<13}{'HeldConceptCos':<17}{'BasisMB_fp16':<15}{'Verdict'}")
print("-" * 96)

rank_rows = []

# Held-out concept centers
held_concept_centers = []
for c in HELD_CONCEPTS:
    idx = [i for i, lab in enumerate(held_concept_labels) if lab == c]
    if idx:
        held_concept_centers.append(Xheld_concept_resp[idx].mean(dim=0))
held_concept_mat = torch.stack(held_concept_centers, dim=0)
held_concept_centered = held_concept_mat - mu

for R in RANKS:
    V = Vh_float[:R]

    amp = Xc @ V.T
    recon = amp @ V
    cal_cos, _ = cos_rows(Xc, recon)

    hc_amp = held_concept_centered @ V.T
    hc_recon = hc_amp @ V
    held_cos, _ = cos_rows(held_concept_centered, hc_recon)

    basis_mb = (R * M * 2) / 1_000_000.0

    if held_cos >= 0.80 and cal_cos >= 0.90:
        verdict = "🟢 STRONG"
    elif held_cos >= 0.65:
        verdict = "🟡 PARTIAL"
    else:
        verdict = "🔴 WEAK"

    rank_rows.append((R, cal_cos, held_cos, basis_mb))
    print(f"{R:<7}{cal_cos:<13.4f}{held_cos:<17.4f}{basis_mb:<15.3f}{verdict}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# TEST 3: Flat Rank-R vs Sequential Residual SVD math proof
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n" + "=" * 96)
print("🧪 TEST 3: FLAT RANK-R vs SEQUENTIAL RESIDUAL SVD MATH PROOF")
print("=" * 96)

R_proof = min(8, eff_rank)
r1 = R_proof // 2
r2 = R_proof - r1

V_flat = Vh_float[:R_proof]
flat_recon = Xc @ V_flat.T @ V_flat

# Sequential residual: first r1, then r2 from residual
V1 = Vh_float[:r1]
proj1 = Xc @ V1.T @ V1
resid = Xc - proj1

with torch.inference_mode():
    _, _, Vh_resid = torch.linalg.svd(resid.double(), full_matrices=False)

V2 = Vh_resid[:r2].float()
seq_recon = proj1 + (resid @ V2.T @ V2)

diff_norm = float((flat_recon - seq_recon).norm().item())
flat_norm = float(flat_recon.norm().item())
rel_diff = diff_norm / max(flat_norm, 1e-12)

print(f"Configuration: Flat Rank-{R_proof} vs Sequential {r1}+{r2}")
print(f"Relative reconstruction difference: {rel_diff:.6e}")

if rel_diff < 1e-4:
    print("✅ MATH PROOF: Sequential residual SVD is numerically equivalent to flat equal-rank SVD.")
    print("   Matlab 'Spiral Zoom beats flat SVD at equal rank' claim is NOT supported by this math.")
else:
    print("⚠️ Sequential residual SVD differs from flat SVD in this setup.")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# TEST 4: Held-out concept wave projection detail
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n" + "=" * 96)
print("🧪 TEST 4: HELD-OUT CONCEPT WAVE GENERALIZATION")
print("=" * 96)

R_held = min(8, eff_rank)
V_held = Vh_float[:R_held]

print(f"{'Concept':<12}{'OrigNorm':<12}{'ReconNorm':<12}{'Cosine':<10}{'Verdict'}")
print("-" * 96)

held_details = []

for i, c in enumerate(HELD_CONCEPTS):
    true_v = held_concept_centered[i]
    amp = true_v @ V_held.T
    recon = amp @ V_held

    tn = float(true_v.norm().item())
    rn = float(recon.norm().item())
    cos = float(F.cosine_similarity(true_v.unsqueeze(0), recon.unsqueeze(0)).item())
    held_details.append(cos)

    verdict = "🟢 OK" if cos >= 0.75 else ("🟡 PARTIAL" if cos >= 0.50 else "🔴 COLLAPSE")
    print(f"{c:<12}{tn:<12.4f}{rn:<12.4f}{cos:<10.4f}{verdict}")

held_avg_cos = float(np.mean(held_details)) if held_details else 0.0
print(f"\nAverage held-out concept wave cosine: {held_avg_cos:.4f}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# TEST 5: Input -> Amplitude Param Generation Router
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n" + "=" * 96)
print("🧪 TEST 5: INPUT -> AMPLITUDE PARAM GENERATION ROUTER")
print("=" * 96)

R_router = min(8, eff_rank)
V_router = Vh_float[:R_router]

amp_cal = Xc @ V_router.T

Xhold_phrase_centered = Xhold_phrase_resp - mu
amp_hold_true = Xhold_phrase_centered @ V_router.T

def ridge_fit_predict(X_train, Y_train, X_test, lam=1e-2):
    Xd = X_train.double()
    Yd = Y_train.double()

    ones_tr = torch.ones(Xd.shape[0], 1, dtype=Xd.dtype)
    Xa = torch.cat([Xd, ones_tr], dim=1)

    XtX = Xa.T @ Xa
    XtY = Xa.T @ Yd
    eye = torch.eye(XtX.shape[0], dtype=Xd.dtype) * lam

    W = torch.linalg.solve(XtX + eye, XtY)

    Xe = X_test.double()
    ones_te = torch.ones(Xe.shape[0], 1, dtype=Xe.dtype)
    Xea = torch.cat([Xe, ones_te], dim=1)

    return (Xea @ W).float()

Xcal_emb_n = F.normalize(Xcal_emb, dim=1)
Xhold_emb_n = F.normalize(Xhold_phrase_emb, dim=1)

amp_pred = ridge_fit_predict(Xcal_emb_n, amp_cal, Xhold_emb_n, lam=1e-2)

amp_cos_mean, amp_cos_vec = cos_rows(amp_hold_true, amp_pred)

print(f"Router rank: {R_router}")
print(f"Held-out phrase amplitude cosine mean: {amp_cos_mean:.4f}")
print("\nPer held-out phrase amplitude cosine:")
for lab, cos in zip(held_phrase_labels, amp_cos_vec.tolist()):
    print(f"  {lab:<10} {cos:.4f}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# TEST 6: Generated Amplitudes -> Concept Classification
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n" + "=" * 96)
print("🧪 TEST 6: GENERATED PARAMS -> CONCEPT BEHAVIOR CLASSIFIER")
print("=" * 96)

cal_concept_ids = torch.tensor([concept_to_id[c] for c in cal_labels], dtype=torch.long)
hold_phrase_ids = torch.tensor([concept_to_id[c] for c in held_phrase_labels], dtype=torch.long)

Yoh = torch.zeros(amp_cal.shape[0], C_all, dtype=torch.float32)
Yoh[torch.arange(amp_cal.shape[0]), cal_concept_ids] = 1.0

logits_from_true_amp = ridge_fit_predict(amp_cal, Yoh, amp_hold_true, lam=1e-2)
logits_from_pred_amp = ridge_fit_predict(amp_cal, Yoh, amp_pred, lam=1e-2)

acc_true = float((logits_from_true_amp.argmax(dim=1) == hold_phrase_ids).float().mean().item())
acc_pred = float((logits_from_pred_amp.argmax(dim=1) == hold_phrase_ids).float().mean().item())

print(f"Accuracy using TRUE teacher amplitudes: {acc_true * 100:.1f}%")
print(f"Accuracy using ROUTER-GENERATED amplitudes: {acc_pred * 100:.1f}%")

print("\nPer held-out phrase prediction:")
pred_ids = logits_from_pred_amp.argmax(dim=1).tolist()
true_ids = hold_phrase_ids.tolist()
id_to_concept = {i: c for c, i in concept_to_id.items()}

for lab, pid, tid in zip(held_phrase_labels, pred_ids, true_ids):
    ok = "🟢" if pid == tid else "🔴"
    print(f"  {ok} true={id_to_concept[tid]:<10} pred={id_to_concept[pid]:<10}")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# TEST 7: 1MB DNA Size Budget
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n" + "=" * 96)
print("🧪 TEST 7: 1MB DNA SIZE BUDGET FOR THIS WAVE-DNA PROTOTYPE")
print("=" * 96)

R_budget = min(8, eff_rank)

basis_params = R_budget * M
router_params = (D + 1) * R_budget
amp_classifier_params = (R_budget + 1) * C_all
stored_amp_params = Xcal_resp.shape[0] * R_budget

total_params = basis_params + router_params + amp_classifier_params + stored_amp_params
fp16_bytes = total_params * 2
fp16_mb = fp16_bytes / 1_000_000.0

print(f"Chosen rank: {R_budget}")
print(f"Wave basis params: {basis_params:,}")
print(f"Router params: {router_params:,}")
print(f"Amplitude classifier params: {amp_classifier_params:,}")
print(f"Stored calibration amplitudes: {stored_amp_params:,}")
print(f"Total params: {total_params:,}")
print(f"fp16 size: {fp16_mb:.3f} MB")

if fp16_mb <= 1.0:
    print("✅ This narrow wave-DNA prototype fits under 1MB.")
else:
    print("🔴 This prototype exceeds 1MB; reduce rank or dimensionality.")

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# FINAL VERDICT
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n" + "=" * 96)
print("🏆 PD-1 FINAL VERDICT")
print("=" * 96)

best_held_rank_cos = max([r[2] for r in rank_rows]) if rank_rows else 0.0

print(f"1. Flat vs Sequential equal-rank difference : {rel_diff:.6e}")
print(f"2. Best held-out concept wave cosine        : {best_held_rank_cos:.4f}")
print(f"3. Router amplitude cosine                  : {amp_cos_mean:.4f}")
print(f"4. True-amplitude concept accuracy          : {acc_true * 100:.1f}%")
print(f"5. Generated-amplitude concept accuracy     : {acc_pred * 100:.1f}%")
print(f"6. DNA prototype size                       : {fp16_mb:.3f} MB")
print()

if rel_diff < 1e-4:
    print("✅ MATH PROVEN: Hierarchical/sequential residual SVD does NOT beat flat SVD at equal rank.")
    print("   Therefore 'Spiral Zoom' is not the magic solution. The real value is wave/function representation.")
else:
    print("⚠️ Sequential residual SVD showed some difference; inspect numerics.")

if best_held_rank_cos >= 0.70:
    print("✅ SUPPORTED: Teacher concept responses can be approximated by a shared low-rank wave basis,")
    print("   even for held-out concepts in this narrow domain.")
else:
    print("🟡 PARTIAL: Wave basis generalization is weak. Need larger calibration or better wave features.")

if amp_cos_mean >= 0.60 and acc_pred >= 0.60:
    print("✅ SUPPORTED: Input prompt can generate amplitude params that recover concept behavior reasonably.")
    print("   This validates the core idea: input + DNA -> temporary params -> behavior.")
else:
    print("🟡 PARTIAL: Input-conditioned param generation is working but not strong enough yet.")

if fp16_mb <= 1.0:
    print("✅ SIZE FEASIBLE: A narrow-domain wave-DNA artifact can fit under 1MB.")
else:
    print("🔴 SIZE ISSUE: Current artifact too large for 1MB target.")

print()
print("IMPORTANT HONEST LIMIT:")
print("  This proves narrow knowledge-wave DNA + input-conditioned param generation.")
print("  It does NOT prove that 1MB can replace a full general LLM with all language/facts/reasoning.")
print("  Next real step: replace teacher embedding input with a tiny DNA-owned encoder,")
print("  then scale from concept classification to actual token generation.")

print(f"\n⏱️ Total time: {time.time() - t0:.1f}s")

# cleanup
del model
gc.collect() ================================================================================================
