        "distractor_test": "Despite iron being Fe, the symbol for copper is",
    },
]

CONCEPT_NAMES = [c["name"] for c in CONCEPTS]
CONCEPT_TO_IDX = {c: i for i, c in enumerate(CONCEPT_NAMES)}
CONCEPT_KIND = {c["name"]: c["kind"] for c in CONCEPTS}
ANSWER_MAP = {c["name"]: c["answer"] for c in CONCEPTS}
N_CONCEPTS = len(CONCEPTS)

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 2. Build train/test rows
# ------------------------------------------------------------
train_rows = []
test_rows = []


def add_row(rows, prompt, concept, typ, split, target_text, mask_terms):
    rows.append({
        "prompt": prompt,
        "concept": concept,
        "type": typ,
        "split": split,
        "target_text": target_text,
        "mask_terms": mask_terms,
    })


for c in CONCEPTS:
    name = c["name"]
    kind = c["kind"]
    answer = c["answer"]
    alias = c["alias"]

    if kind == "country":
        direct_train = [
            f"The capital of {name} is",
            f"{name}'s capital is",
            f"Please name the capital of {name}:",
        ]
        direct_test = [
            f"What is the capital of {name}?",
        ]

        alias_train = [
            f"The capital of {alias} is",
        ]
        alias_test = [
            f"Tell me the capital of {alias}.",
        ]

        clue_train = [
            f"{c['clue_train']}, its capital is",
        ]
        clue_test = [
            f"{c['clue_test']}, its capital is",
        ]

        reverse_train = [
            c["reverse_train"],
        ]
        reverse_test = [
            c["reverse_test"],
        ]

        distractor_train = [
            c["distractor_train"],
        ]
        distractor_test = [
            c["distractor_test"],
        ]

        target_forward = answer
        target_reverse = name

    else:
        direct_train = [
            f"The chemical symbol for {name} is",
            f"{name} has the chemical symbol",
            f"Please give the chemical symbol for {name}:",
        ]
        direct_test = [
            f"What is the chemical symbol for {name}?",
        ]

        alias_train = [
            f"The chemical symbol for {alias} is",
        ]
        alias_test = [
            f"State the symbol for {alias}.",
        ]

        clue_train = [
            f"{c['clue_train']}, its symbol is",
        ]
        clue_test = [
            f"{c['clue_test']}, its symbol is",
        ]

        reverse_train = [
            c["reverse_train"],
        ]
        reverse_test = [
            c["reverse_test"],
        ]

        distractor_train = [
            c["distractor_train"],
        ]
        distractor_test = [
            c["distractor_test"],
        ]

        target_forward = answer
        target_reverse = name

    mask_terms = [name]

    for p in direct_train:
        add_row(train_rows, p, name, "direct", "train", target_forward, mask_terms)
    for p in direct_test:
        add_row(test_rows, p, name, "direct", "test", target_forward, mask_terms)

    for p in alias_train:
        add_row(train_rows, p, name, "alias", "train", target_forward, mask_terms)
    for p in alias_test:
        add_row(test_rows, p, name, "alias", "test", target_forward, mask_terms)

    for p in clue_train:
        add_row(train_rows, p, name, "clue", "train", target_forward, mask_terms)
    for p in clue_test:
        add_row(test_rows, p, name, "clue", "test", target_forward, mask_terms)

    for p in reverse_train:
        add_row(train_rows, p, name, "reverse", "train", target_reverse, mask_terms)
    for p in reverse_test:
        add_row(test_rows, p, name, "reverse", "test", target_reverse, mask_terms)

    for p in distractor_train:
        add_row(train_rows, p, name, "distractor", "train", target_forward, mask_terms)
    for p in distractor_test:
        add_row(test_rows, p, name, "distractor", "test", target_forward, mask_terms)

all_rows = train_rows + test_rows

print(f"🧠 Train prompts: {len(train_rows)}")
print(f"🧪 Test paraphrase prompts: {len(test_rows)}")
print(f"🧩 Concepts stored in DNA: {N_CONCEPTS}")


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 3. DNA-owned hash encoder + masking
# ------------------------------------------------------------
def char_hash_features(text, dim=HASH_DIM):
    text = text.lower()
    feats = np.zeros(dim, dtype=np.float32)

    def add(gram):
        h = int(hashlib.md5(gram.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest(), 16)
        idx = h % dim
        sign = 1.0 if ((h // dim) & 1) == 0 else -1.0
        feats[idx] += sign

    for n in (2, 3, 4):
        for i in range(max(0, len(text) - n + 1)):
            add(text[i:i + n])

    clean = (
        text.replace("'", " ")
            .replace(",", " ")
            .replace(":", " ")
            .replace("?", " ")
            .replace("!", " ")
            .replace(".", " ")
    )
    for w in clean.split():
        if len(w) > 1:
            add("W" + w)
            if len(w) > 3:
                add("P" + w[:3])
                add("S" + w[-3:])

    norm = np.linalg.norm(feats)
    if norm > 1e-8:
        feats = feats / norm

    return torch.from_numpy(feats)


def mask_text(text, terms):
    t = text.lower()
    for term in sorted(terms, key=len, reverse=True):
        t = t.replace(term.lower(), " ")
    return t


for r in all_rows:
    r["hash_raw"] = char_hash_features(r["prompt"])
    r["hash_masked"] = char_hash_features(mask_text(r["prompt"], r["mask_terms"]))


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 4. Symbolic n-gram index memory
# ------------------------------------------------------------
STOPWORDS = {
    "the", "a", "an", "of", "in", "on", "to", "for", "and", "or", "not",
    "is", "are", "was", "were", "be", "been", "being", "it", "its",
    "which", "what", "please", "name", "give", "state", "tell", "me",
    "has", "have", "had", "although", "despite", "official", "capital",
    "country", "nation", "element", "chemical", "symbol", "represents",
    "serves", "called", "known", "home", "contains", "hosted", "used",
    "widely", "common", "highly", "reddish", "dense", "precious",
    "metal", "latin", "name", "atomic", "number", "whose", "that",
    "this", "with", "without", "from", "by", "as", "at", "if", "then",
}


def basic_tokens(text):
    text = text.lower()
    out = []
    for ch in text:
        if ch.isalnum():
            out.append(ch)
        else:
            out.append(" ")
    return "".join(out).split()


def make_ngrams(tokens, ns=(1, 2, 3)):
    grams = set()
    for n in ns:
        if len(tokens) < n:
            continue
        for i in range(len(tokens) - n + 1):
            grams.add(" ".join(tokens[i:i + n]))
    return grams


def build_atom_index_grams(c):
    """
    Index grams are stored in DNA as symbolic memory.
    Concept name is excluded from alias/clue grams to reduce lexical shortcut.
    Answer grams are kept intact because reverse queries need answer -> concept.
    """
    excl = set(basic_tokens(c["name"]))
    grams = set()

    # Answer grams: keep all tokens, e.g. "mexico city", "new delhi"
    ans_toks = basic_tokens(c["answer"])
    grams.update(make_ngrams(ans_toks))

    # Alias grams: exclude concept-name tokens and stopwords
    alias_toks = [
        t for t in basic_tokens(c["alias"])
        if t not in STOPWORDS and t not in excl
    ]
    grams.update(make_ngrams(alias_toks))

    # Clue grams: exclude concept-name tokens and stopwords
    clue_toks = [
        t for t in basic_tokens(c["clue_train"])
        if t not in STOPWORDS and t not in excl
    ]
    grams.update(make_ngrams(clue_toks))

    if not grams:
        grams.update(make_ngrams(ans_toks))

    return grams


concept_grams = {c["name"]: build_atom_index_grams(c) for c in CONCEPTS}
all_index_grams = set()
for gset in concept_grams.values():
    all_index_grams.update(gset)

# IDF over stored atom grams
df = defaultdict(int)
for gset in concept_grams.values():
    for g in gset:
        df[g] += 1

idf = {}
for g, cnt in df.items():
    idf[g] = math.log((N_CONCEPTS + 1) / (cnt + 1)) + 1.0

concept_norm2 = {}
for name, gset in concept_grams.items():
    concept_norm2[name] = float(sum(idf[g] * idf[g] for g in gset))


def prompt_index_grams(text):
    toks = basic_tokens(text)
    ng = make_ngrams(toks)
    return ng & all_index_grams


for r in all_rows:
    r["prompt_grams"] = prompt_index_grams(r["prompt"])
    r["prompt_norm2"] = float(sum(idf[g] * idf[g] for g in r["prompt_grams"]))


def index_score(row, atom):
    pg = row["prompt_grams"]
    ag = atom["grams"]
    if not pg or not ag:
        return 0.0

    inter = pg & ag
    if not inter:
        return 0.0

    num = float(sum(idf[g] for g in inter))
    den = math.sqrt(max(row["prompt_norm2"], 1e-12) * max(atom["norm2"], 1e-12))
    if den <= 1e-12:
        return 0.0
    return max(0.0, min(1.0, num / den))


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 5. Load teacher model for extraction only
# ------------------------------------------------------------
try:
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
        MODEL,
        dtype=torch.float32,
        low_cpu_mem_usage=True
    )
except TypeError:
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(
        MODEL,
        torch_dtype=torch.float32,
        low_cpu_mem_usage=True
    )

tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL)
tok.padding_side = "left"
if tok.pad_token is None:
    tok.pad_token = tok.eos_token

model.eval()

NL = model.config.num_hidden_layers
D = model.config.hidden_size

SEL_LAYERS = [l for l in SELECTED_LAYER_CANDIDATES if l < NL]
M = len(SEL_LAYERS) * D

W_unembed = model.get_output_embeddings().weight.detach().float()

print(f"✅ Teacher loaded for extraction | layers={NL} | selected_layers={len(SEL_LAYERS)} | hidden={D}")
print(f"📐 Selected layers: {SEL_LAYERS}")
print(f"📐 Wave response dimension: {M}")


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 6. Capture teacher activation waves for TRAIN rows only
# ------------------------------------------------------------
def capture_teacher_rows(rows, batch_size=4):
    for start in range(0, len(rows), batch_size):
        batch = rows[start:start + batch_size]
        prompts = [r["prompt"] for r in batch]

        inp = tok(
            prompts,
            return_tensors="pt",
            padding=True,
            truncation=True,
            max_length=64
        )

        with torch.inference_mode():
            out = model(**inp, output_hidden_states=True)

        hs = out.hidden_states

        for b, r in enumerate(batch):
            target_ids = tok(r["target_text"], add_special_tokens=False).input_ids
            target_id = target_ids[0] if target_ids else tok("<unk>").input_ids[0]
            r["target_id"] = int(target_id)

            H = torch.stack(
                [hs[l + 1][b, -1, :].float() for l in SEL_LAYERS],
                dim=0
            )  # (len(SEL_LAYERS), D)

            r["H"] = H.reshape(-1).cpu()


print("\n📐 Capturing teacher activation waves for train rows...")
capture_teacher_rows(train_rows)
print(f"✅ Capture done ({time.time() - t0:.1f}s)")


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 7. Build per-domain wave bases from TRAIN rows only
# ------------------------------------------------------------
def build_basis(rows, desired_rank, domain_name):
    if len(rows) == 0:
        return {
            "mu": torch.zeros(M, dtype=torch.float32),
            "V": torch.zeros((1, M), dtype=torch.float32),
            "V_last": torch.zeros((1, D), dtype=torch.float32),
            "R": 1,
        }

    X = torch.stack([r["H"] for r in rows], dim=0)
    mu = X.mean(dim=0)
    Xc = X - mu

    with torch.inference_mode():
        _, S, Vh = torch.linalg.svd(Xc.double(), full_matrices=False)

    if S.numel() > 0 and float(S[0]) > 0:
        tol = max(Xc.shape) * float(S[0]) * 1e-12
        eff_rank = int((S > tol).sum().item())
    else:
        eff_rank = 0

    max_possible = min(Vh.shape[0], M)
    R = min(desired_rank, max(1, eff_rank, max_possible))
    R = max(1, min(R, max_possible))

    V = Vh[:R].float()
    last_start = (len(SEL_LAYERS) - 1) * D
    V_last = V[:, last_start:last_start + D].contiguous()

    print(f"   {domain_name}: n={len(rows)} | effective_rank={eff_rank} | used_rank={R}")

    return {
        "mu": mu,
        "V": V,
        "V_last": V_last,
        "R": R,
    }


country_train = [r for r in train_rows if CONCEPT_KIND[r["concept"]] == "country"]
chemical_train = [r for r in train_rows if CONCEPT_KIND[r["concept"]] == "chemical"]

print("\n🧠 Building per-domain wave bases...")
basis_c = build_basis(country_train, R_COUNTRY_DESIRED, "country")
basis_k = build_basis(chemical_train, R_CHEMICAL_DESIRED, "chemical")

R_C = int(basis_c["R"])
R_K = int(basis_k["R"])

print(f"✅ Wave bases ready | country_rank={R_C} | chemical_rank={R_K} | response_dim={M}")


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 8. Compute train-row wave amplitudes and output sensitivity
# ------------------------------------------------------------
for r in train_rows:
    kind = CONCEPT_KIND[r["concept"]]
    basis = basis_c if kind == "country" else basis_k

    r["amp"] = ((r["H"] - basis["mu"]) @ basis["V"].T).float().cpu()

    w_target = W_unembed[r["target_id"]].float()
    r["sens"] = (basis["V_last"] @ w_target).float().cpu()


# Free large unembed reference before deleting model.
del W_unembed


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 9. Build DNA atoms from TRAIN rows
# ------------------------------------------------------------
def mean_tensor(rows, key, dim):
    if len(rows) == 0:
        return torch.zeros(dim, dtype=torch.float32)
    return torch.stack([r[key] for r in rows], dim=0).mean(dim=0).float().cpu()


atoms = []

for c in CONCEPTS:
    name = c["name"]
    kind = c["kind"]
    R = R_C if kind == "country" else R_K

    rs = [r for r in train_rows if r["concept"] == name]
    fwd = [r for r in rs if r["type"] != "reverse"]
    rev = [r for r in rs if r["type"] == "reverse"]
    mask_rs = [r for r in rs if r["type"] != "direct"]
    if not mask_rs:
        mask_rs = rs

    atom = {
        "concept": name,
        "answer": c["answer"],
        "kind": kind,
        "idx": CONCEPT_TO_IDX[name],
        "R": R,

        "amp": mean_tensor(rs, "amp", R),
        "sens_f": mean_tensor(fwd, "sens", R),
        "sens_r": mean_tensor(rev, "sens", R),

        "hash_raw": mean_tensor(rs, "hash_raw", HASH_DIM),
        "hash_masked": mean_tensor(mask_rs, "hash_masked", HASH_DIM),

        "grams": concept_grams[name],
        "norm2": concept_norm2[name],
    }

    atoms.append(atom)

print(f"✅ DNA atoms built: {len(atoms)} concepts")


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 10. Train DNA-only routers: hash -> wave/sensitivity features
# ------------------------------------------------------------
def ridge_fit(X, Y, lam=1e-2, out_dim=None):
    if X.shape[0] == 0:
        od = out_dim if out_dim is not None else (Y.shape[1] if Y.ndim > 1 and Y.shape[0] > 0 else 0)
        return torch.zeros((X.shape[1] + 1, od), dtype=torch.float32)

    X = X.double()
    Y = Y.double()

    bias = torch.ones(X.shape[0], 1, dtype=X.dtype)
    Xa = torch.cat([X, bias], dim=1)

    A = Xa.T @ Xa
    A = A + lam * torch.eye(A.shape[0], dtype=X.dtype)

    B = Xa.T @ Y

    try:
        W = torch.linalg.solve(A, B)
    except Exception:
        W = torch.linalg.lstsq(A, B).solution

    return W.float()


def ridge_predict(X, W):
    if W.shape[1] == 0:
        return torch.zeros((X.shape[0], 0), dtype=torch.float32)

    X = X.double()
    bias = torch.ones(X.shape[0], 1, dtype=X.dtype)
    Xa = torch.cat([X, bias], dim=1)
    return (Xa @ W.double()).float()


def stack_tensor(rows, key, dim):
    if len(rows) == 0:
        return torch.zeros((0, dim), dtype=torch.float32)
    return torch.stack([r[key] for r in rows], dim=0).float()


# Masked routers should not train on direct masked prompts,
# because direct masked prompts become nearly empty/uninformative.
country_mask_train = [r for r in country_train if r["type"] != "direct"]
chemical_mask_train = [r for r in chemical_train if r["type"] != "direct"]

# Raw routers can use all train rows.
Xc_raw = stack_tensor(country_train, "hash_raw", HASH_DIM)
Xc_mask = stack_tensor(country_mask_train, "hash_masked", HASH_DIM)
Xk_raw = stack_tensor(chemical_train, "hash_raw", HASH_DIM)
Xk_mask = stack_tensor(chemical_mask_train, "hash_masked", HASH_DIM)

Yamp_c = stack_tensor(country_train, "amp", R_C)
Yamp_k = stack_tensor(chemical_train, "amp", R_K)

Ysens_c = stack_tensor(country_train, "sens", R_C)
Ysens_k = stack_tensor(chemical_train, "sens", R_K)

Ysens_c_mask = stack_tensor(country_mask_train, "sens", R_C)
Ysens_k_mask = stack_tensor(chemical_mask_train, "sens", R_K)

Yamp_c_mask = stack_tensor(country_mask_train, "amp", R_C)
Yamp_k_mask = stack_tensor(chemical_mask_train, "amp", R_K)

W_amp_c_raw = ridge_fit(Xc_raw, Yamp_c, lam=1e-2, out_dim=R_C)
W_amp_c_mask = ridge_fit(Xc_mask, Yamp_c_mask, lam=1e-2, out_dim=R_C)

W_amp_k_raw = ridge_fit(Xk_raw, Yamp_k, lam=1e-2, out_dim=R_K)
W_amp_k_mask = ridge_fit(Xk_mask, Yamp_k_mask, lam=1e-2, out_dim=R_K)

W_sens_c_raw = ridge_fit(Xc_raw, Ysens_c, lam=1e-2, out_dim=R_C)
W_sens_c_mask = ridge_fit(Xc_mask, Ysens_c_mask, lam=1e-2, out_dim=R_C)

W_sens_k_raw = ridge_fit(Xk_raw, Ysens_k, lam=1e-2, out_dim=R_K)
W_sens_k_mask = ridge_fit(Xk_mask, Ysens_k_mask, lam=1e-2, out_dim=R_K)

print("✅ DNA-only raw + masked routers trained")


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 11. Delete teacher model. Inference is DNA-only from here.
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n🧹 Deleting teacher model. Inference will use DNA arrays only...")

del model
del tok
gc.collect()

print(f"✅ Teacher deleted. Memory freed. ({time.time() - t0:.1f}s)")


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 12. Lens definitions and DNA-only scoring
# ------------------------------------------------------------
LENS_NAMES = [
    "raw_hash",
    "masked_hash",
    "wave_raw",
    "wave_masked",
    "sensF_raw",
    "sensF_masked",
    "sensR_raw",
    "sensR_masked",
    "index",
]
LENS_TO_IDX = {n: i for i, n in enumerate(LENS_NAMES)}
N_LENS = len(LENS_NAMES)


def cos_1d(a, b):
    a = a.float().reshape(-1)
    b = b.float().reshape(-1)
    if a.numel() != b.numel():
        return 0.0
    na = float(a.norm().item())
    nb = float(b.norm().item())
    if na < 1e-8 or nb < 1e-8:
        return 0.0
    return float((a * b).sum().item() / (na * nb))


def unit_cos(x):
    if not np.isfinite(x):
        return 0.0
    return max(0.0, min(1.0, (float(x) + 1.0) * 0.5))


def compute_row_lens(row):
    hraw = row["hash_raw"].float()
    hmask = row["hash_masked"].float()

    # Domain-specific router predictions
    pred_amp_c_raw = ridge_predict(hraw.unsqueeze(0), W_amp_c_raw).squeeze(0)
    pred_amp_c_mask = ridge_predict(hmask.unsqueeze(0), W_amp_c_mask).squeeze(0)
    pred_sens_c_raw = ridge_predict(hraw.unsqueeze(0), W_sens_c_raw).squeeze(0)
    pred_sens_c_mask = ridge_predict(hmask.unsqueeze(0), W_sens_c_mask).squeeze(0)

    pred_amp_k_raw = ridge_predict(hraw.unsqueeze(0), W_amp_k_raw).squeeze(0)
    pred_amp_k_mask = ridge_predict(hmask.unsqueeze(0), W_amp_k_mask).squeeze(0)
    pred_sens_k_raw = ridge_predict(hraw.unsqueeze(0), W_sens_k_raw).squeeze(0)
    pred_sens_k_mask = ridge_predict(hmask.unsqueeze(0), W_sens_k_mask).squeeze(0)

    lens_raw = torch.zeros(len(atoms), N_LENS, dtype=torch.float32)
    lens_norm = torch.zeros(len(atoms), N_LENS, dtype=torch.float32)

    for ai, atom in enumerate(atoms):
        if atom["kind"] == "country":
            pa_raw = pred_amp_c_raw
            pa_mask = pred_amp_c_mask
            ps_raw = pred_sens_c_raw
            ps_mask = pred_sens_c_mask
        else:
            pa_raw = pred_amp_k_raw
            pa_mask = pred_amp_k_mask
            ps_raw = pred_sens_k_raw
            ps_mask = pred_sens_k_mask

        vals = [
            cos_1d(hraw, atom["hash_raw"]),
            cos_1d(hmask, atom["hash_masked"]),
            cos_1d(pa_raw, atom["amp"]),
            cos_1d(pa_mask, atom["amp"]),
            cos_1d(ps_raw, atom["sens_f"]),
            cos_1d(ps_mask, atom["sens_f"]),
            cos_1d(ps_raw, atom["sens_r"]),
            cos_1d(ps_mask, atom["sens_r"]),
            index_score(row, atom),
        ]

        for j, v in enumerate(vals):
            lens_raw[ai, j] = float(v)
            if j < 8:
                lens_norm[ai, j] = unit_cos(v)
            else:
                lens_norm[ai, j] = max(0.0, min(1.0, float(v)))

    row["lens_scores_raw"] = lens_raw
    row["lens_scores_norm"] = lens_norm


for r in all_rows:
    compute_row_lens(r)

print("✅ DNA-only lens scores computed for all rows")


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 13. Scalable linear fusion trainer
# ------------------------------------------------------------
def train_linear_fusion(rows, lens_mask, steps=450, lr=0.12, wd=1e-3):
    if len(rows) == 0:
        return torch.zeros(len(lens_mask)), torch.zeros(len(atoms))

    X = torch.stack([r["lens_scores_norm"][:, lens_mask] for r in rows], dim=0)  # (n,A,L)
    y = torch.tensor([CONCEPT_TO_IDX[r["concept"]] for r in rows], dtype=torch.long)

    L = len(lens_mask)
    A = len(atoms)

    w = torch.zeros(L, dtype=torch.float32, requires_grad=True)
    b = torch.zeros(A, dtype=torch.float32, requires_grad=True)

    opt = torch.optim.Adam([w, b], lr=lr)

    for _ in range(steps):
        opt.zero_grad()
        logits = torch.einsum("nal,l->na", X, w) + b.unsqueeze(0)
        loss = F.cross_entropy(logits, y) + wd * (w.pow(2).sum() + b.pow(2).sum())
        loss.backward()
        opt.step()

    return w.detach(), b.detach()


CLOSED_MASK = [
    LENS_TO_IDX["masked_hash"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["wave_masked"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["sensF_masked"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["sensR_masked"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["index"],
]

NO_INDEX_MASK = [
    LENS_TO_IDX["masked_hash"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["wave_masked"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["sensF_masked"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["sensR_masked"],
]

OPEN_MASK = [
    LENS_TO_IDX["raw_hash"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["wave_raw"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["sensF_raw"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["sensR_raw"],
    LENS_TO_IDX["index"],
]

ALL_MASK = list(range(N_LENS))

# For closed-book fusion training, exclude direct train rows because
# direct masked prompts are intentionally uninformative.
fusion_train_closed = [r for r in train_rows if r["type"] != "direct"]

LINEAR_MODELS = {}

LINEAR_MODELS["hybrid_closed"] = train_linear_fusion(fusion_train_closed, CLOSED_MASK)
LINEAR_MODELS["hybrid_no_index"] = train_linear_fusion(fusion_train_closed, NO_INDEX_MASK)
LINEAR_MODELS["hybrid_open"] = train_linear_fusion(train_rows, OPEN_MASK)
LINEAR_MODELS["all_linear"] = train_linear_fusion(train_rows, ALL_MASK)

print("✅ Scalable linear fusion models trained")


# ------------------------------------------------------------
# 14. Prediction specs
# ------------------------------------------------------------
MODE_SPECS = {}

for lens in LENS_NAMES:
    MODE_SPECS[f"lens_{lens}"] = ("lens", LENS_TO_IDX[lens])

MODE_SPECS["hybrid_closed"] = ("linear", "hybrid_closed", CLOSED_MASK)
MODE_SPECS["hybrid_no_index"] = ("linear", "hybrid_no_index", NO_INDEX_MASK)
MODE_SPECS["hybrid_open"] = ("linear", "hybrid_open", OPEN_MASK)
MODE_SPECS["all_linear"] = ("linear", "all_linear", ALL_MASK)


def predict_spec(row, spec):
    if spec[0] == "lens":
        idx = spec[1]
        scores = row["lens_scores_raw"][:, idx]
        best = int(torch.argmax(scores).item())
        return atoms[best]["concept"], scores

    model_key = spec[1]
    lens_mask = spec[2]
    w, b = LINEAR_MODELS[model_key]
    logits = row["lens_scores_norm"][:, lens_mask] @ w + b
    best = int(torch.argmax(logits).item())
    return atoms[best]["concept"], logits


def display_target_for_prompt(row, pred_concept):
    if row["type"] == "reverse":
        return pred_concept
    return ANSWER_MAP.get(pred_concept, "?")

    f"{'Alias%':<10}{'Clue%':<10}{'Reverse%':<11}{'Distractor%'}"
)

mode_results = {}

for mode_name, spec in MODE_SPECS.items():
    type_correct = {t: 0 for t in TYPE_ORDER}
    type_total = {t: 0 for t in TYPE_ORDER}
    overall_correct = 0
    fair_correct = 0
    fair_total = 0

    for r in test_rows:
        pred, _ = predict_spec(r, spec)
        typ = r["type"]
        type_total[typ] += 1

        ok = (pred == r["concept"])
        if ok:
            overall_correct += 1
            type_correct[typ] += 1

        if typ in FAIR_TYPES:
            fair_total += 1
            fair_correct += int(ok)

    overall_acc = overall_correct / max(1, len(test_rows))
    fair_acc = fair_correct / max(1, fair_total)
    type_acc = {t: type_correct[t] / max(1, type_total[t]) for t in TYPE_ORDER}

    mode_results[mode_name] = {
        "overall": overall_acc,
        "fair_closed": fair_acc,
        "types": type_acc,
    }

    print(
        f"{mode_name:<24}{overall_acc * 100:<11.1f}{fair_acc * 100:<13.1f}"
        f"{type_acc['direct'] * 100:<10.1f}{type_acc['alias'] * 100:<10.1f}"
        f"{type_acc['clue'] * 100:<10.1f}{type_acc['reverse'] * 100:<11.1f}"
        f"{type_acc['distractor'] * 100:.1f}"
    )

# ------------------------------------------------------------
# TEST 4: Discriminative margin by prompt type — hybrid_closed
# ------------------------------------------------------------
print("\n" + "=" * 112)
print("🧪 TEST 4: DISCRIMINATIVE MARGIN — hybrid_closed")
print("   Margin = softmax(top1) - softmax(top2)")
print("=" * 112)

margin_by_type = {t: [] for t in TYPE_ORDER}

for r in test_rows:
    _, logits = predict_spec(r, MODE_SPECS["hybrid_closed"])
    probs = F.softmax(logits, dim=0)
    top2 = torch.topk(probs, 2)
    margin = float((top2.values[0] - top2.values[1]).item())
    margin_by_type[r["type"]].append(margin)

print(f"{'Type':<14}{'AvgMargin':<12}{'MinMargin':<12}{'MaxMargin':<12}{'Verdict'}")
print("-" * 78)

for t in TYPE_ORDER:
    vals = margin_by_type[t]
    if not vals:
        continue
    avg = float(np.mean(vals))
    mn = float(np.min(vals))
    mx = float(np.max(vals))
    verdict = "🟢" if avg >= 0.20 else ("🟡" if avg >= 0.08 else "🔴")
    print(f"{t:<14}{avg:<12.4f}{mn:<12.4f}{mx:<12.4f}{verdict}")

UNKNOWN_PROMPTS = [
    "The capital of Atlantis is",
    "The capital of Nowhereland is",
    "The chemical symbol for Unobtainium is",
    "The chemical symbol for Fictionium is",
    "The capital of Moonville is",
    "Which element has atomic number 999",
]

print("-" * 112)

unknown_margins = []

for p in UNKNOWN_PROMPTS:
    row = {
        "prompt": p,
        "concept": None,
        "type": "unknown",
        "split": "unknown",
        "target_text": "",
        "mask_terms": [],
    }
    row["hash_raw"] = char_hash_features(p)
    row["hash_masked"] = char_hash_features(mask_text(p, []))
    row["prompt_grams"] = prompt_index_grams(p)
    row["prompt_norm2"] = float(sum(idf[g] * idf[g] for g in row["prompt_grams"]))

    compute_row_lens(row)

    pred_fusion, logits = predict_spec(row, MODE_SPECS["hybrid_closed"])
    probs = F.softmax(logits, dim=0)
    top2 = torch.topk(probs, 2)
    fusion_margin = float((top2.values[0] - top2.values[1]).item())
    unknown_margins.append(fusion_margin)

    idx_scores = row["lens_scores_raw"][:, LENS_TO_IDX["index"]]
    idx_best = int(torch.argmax(idx_scores).item())
    idx_pred = atoms[idx_best]["concept"]

    if fusion_margin < 0.05:
        status = "🟡 LOW CONFIDENCE"
    elif fusion_margin < 0.15:
        status = "🟠 WEAK CONFIDENCE"
    else:
        status = "🔴 OVERCONFIDENT"

    short_p = p if len(p) <= 48 else p[:45] + "..."
    print(
        f"{short_p:<50}{pred_fusion:<14}{idx_pred:<14}"
        f"{fusion_margin:<15.4f}{status}"
    )

avg_unknown_margin = float(np.mean(unknown_margins)) if unknown_margins else 0.0
print(f"\nAverage unknown fusion margin: {avg_unknown_margin:.4f}")
print("Note: Unknown concepts are not in DNA memory. Good DNA should show low margin.")

basis_params = (R_C + R_K + 2) * M
router_params = 4 * (R_C + R_K) * (HASH_DIM + 1)

atom_numeric_closed = 0
atom_numeric_open_extra = 0
for atom in atoms:
    R = atom["R"]
    atom_numeric_closed += 3 * R + HASH_DIM  # amp, sens_f, sens_r, masked_hash
    atom_numeric_open_extra += HASH_DIM     # optional raw_hash centroid

index_bytes = sum(len(atom["grams"]) for atom in atoms) * 4
text_bytes = sum(len(c["name"]) + len(c["answer"]) + len(c["alias"]) for c in CONCEPTS)

fusion_params_closed = (len(CLOSED_MASK) + 1) * N_CONCEPTS
meta_params = 128

numeric_closed = basis_params + router_params + atom_numeric_closed + fusion_params_closed + meta_params
numeric_open_extra = atom_numeric_open_extra

fp16_closed_mb = (numeric_closed * 2 + index_bytes + text_bytes) / 1_000_000.0
int8_closed_mb = (numeric_closed + index_bytes + text_bytes) / 1_000_000.0

fp16_open_mb = ((numeric_closed + numeric_open_extra) * 2 + index_bytes + text_bytes) / 1_000_000.0
int8_open_mb = (numeric_closed + numeric_open_extra + index_bytes + text_bytes) / 1_000_000.0

avg_grams = float(np.mean([len(atom["grams"]) for atom in atoms]))

print(f"Selected layers                 : {len(SEL_LAYERS)}")
print(f"Wave response dimension         : {M}")
print(f"Country wave rank               : {R_C}")
print(f"Chemical wave rank              : {R_K}")
print(f"Number of DNA atoms             : {N_CONCEPTS}")
print(f"Hash encoder dim                : {HASH_DIM}")
print(f"Average index grams per atom    : {avg_grams:.1f}")
print()
print(f"Wave basis params               : {basis_params:,}")
print(f"Router params                   : {router_params:,}")
print(f"Atom numeric params closed      : {atom_numeric_closed:,}")
print(f"Optional raw-hash atom params   : {atom_numeric_open_extra:,}")
print(f"Fusion linear params closed     : {fusion_params_closed:,}")
print(f"Index bytes rough               : {index_bytes:,}")
print(f"Text bytes rough                : {text_bytes:,}")
print()
print(f"Closed-book fp16 estimated size : {fp16_closed_mb:.3f} MB")
print(f"Closed-book int8 estimated size : {int8_closed_mb:.3f} MB")
print(f"Open-book extra fp16 size       : {fp16_open_mb:.3f} MB")
print(f"Open-book extra int8 size       : {int8_open_mb:.3f} MB")

if int8_closed_mb <= 1.0:
    print("✅ This PD-6 10-concept closed-book DNA prototype fits under 1MB in int8 estimate.")
else:
    print("🔴 Current closed-book prototype exceeds 1MB int8 estimate.")


def estimate_scale(N_atoms, hash_dim, avg_R=8.0):
    fixed_basis = basis_params
    fixed_router = 4 * (R_C + R_K) * (hash_dim + 1)

    atom_numeric = N_atoms * (3 * avg_R + hash_dim)
    fusion = N_atoms * (len(CLOSED_MASK) + 1)

    numeric = fixed_basis + fixed_router + atom_numeric + fusion + meta_params
    index = N_atoms * avg_grams * 4
    text = N_atoms * 80

    fp16_mb = (numeric * 2 + index + text) / 1_000_000.0
    int8_mb = (numeric + index + text) / 1_000_000.0
    return fp16_mb, int8_mb


print("\n📈 Rough scale estimate for closed-book DNA cartridge:")
print(f"{'Atoms':<10}{'HashDim':<10}{'fp16 MB':<12}{'int8 MB':<12}{'Under 1MB int8?'}")
print("-" * 62)

for N in [10, 100, 1000, 5000]:
    for hd in [256, 128]:
        fp16_mb, int8_mb = estimate_scale(N, hd, avg_R=8.0)
        ok = "✅" if int8_mb <= 1.0 else "🔴"
        print(f"{N:<10}{hd:<10}{fp16_mb:<12.3f}{int8_mb:<12.3f}{ok}")

print("\nNote: Scale estimate is size-only. Accuracy at 1000+ atoms requires larger calibration and real tests.")

HELIX PROJECT — CONTINUATION HANDOFF
What exists, what was audited, what must be fixed, what comes next
================================================================

You are continuing work on HELIX, a parametric knowledge-memory
prototype for the "Reverse DNA" project. The previous session
produced a working prototype and a design document. A second
AI reviewer has now audited all code, results, and claims.

This prompt gives you:
  1. Current state summary
  2. Audit findings (what is real, what is misleading)
  3. Specific bugs and gaps to fix
  4. Priority-ordered next steps
  5. Success criteria

Read everything before writing any code.

============================================================
SECTION 1: WHAT EXISTS RIGHT NOW
============================================================

Files produced in the previous session:

  helix.py          Core engine: GF(256) Reed-Solomon codec,
                    WaveLattice (content-addressed slots),
                    RuleEngine (inverse/chain/alias/generative),
                    KnowledgeDNA (compile, encode, retrieve,
                    decode, save/load), transient parameter
                    generation, unknown rejection.

  world.py          Two domains:
                    Domain A: capitals + 118 chemical elements
                    Domain B: 4096 procedurally generated provinces

  evaluate.py       Evaluation harness: accuracy by category,
                    unknown rejection, bits-per-claim,
                    coverage-matched baselines (int8/fp16 teacher,
                    naive row store, brief baseline).

  run_experiment.py Driver that builds both domains, runs
                    evaluation, writes RESULTS.md and results.json.

  DESIGN.md         Full 9-section design proposal.
  RESULTS.md        Measured output.
  README.md         Project overview.

Measured results from previous session:

  Domain A (fact-dense):
    artifact: 12,746 B
    stored atoms: 567
    answerable tuples: 449
    queries: 869, accuracy: 99.9%
    unknown probes: 12, false accepts: 0
    knowledge bits/stored bit: 0.105
    vs int8 teacher: 0.42x (LOSS)

  Domain B (rule-dense):
    artifact: 3,363 B
    stored atoms: 32
    answerable tuples: 4,128
    queries: 12,288, accuracy: 100%
    knowledge bits/stored bit: 12.0
    vs int8 teacher: 47.9x (WIN)

============================================================
SECTION 2: AUDIT FINDINGS — WHAT IS REAL vs MISLEADING
============================================================

A second AI reviewer audited all files. These are the findings:

--- REAL AND VALUABLE ---

1. D_derive insight is correct.
   The multiplier comes from "claims per stored atom",
   not from encoding tricks. Domain B's 47.9x win is
   genuine but it is program compression, not knowledge
   density compression.

2. Unknown rejection design is good.
   12/12 unknown probes rejected with 0 false accepts.
   The three-gate design (structural, algebraic, statistical)
   is a real product feature.

3. Content addressing removes index cost.
   Address = hash(entity, relation, level). No stored keys.
   This is a legitimate size win.

4. Allen-Zhu & Li baseline (2 bits/param) is correctly cited.
   The interpreter tax framing (4-8x) is scientifically sound.

5. Domain A honesty is good.
   The 0.42x loss is reported openly. This builds credibility.

6. Spiral collision fix was a real bug fix.
   6.25% of facts were being silently overwritten before
   the multi-level spiral addressing was added.

--- MISLEADING OR OVERSTATED ---

1. "Wave lattice" branding is misleading.
   The actual slot record is:
     [ent_id u16][level u8][n_facts u8][(rel,objref)...][RS parity]
   This is packed structured bytes, not wave interference.
   The complex number in WaveState:
     wave = np.array([complex(r, o)], dtype=np.complex64)
   is cosmetic. Retrieval is exact hash lookup.

   ACTION: Either implement real wave interference
   (superposition, demodulation, SNR measurement) or
   rename the component honestly as "content-addressed
   slot lattice" and remove wave terminology.

2. "Transient parameters" are decorative.
   theta = A @ z where A is a seeded random matrix.
   theta only selects among 4 hardcoded templates:
     "The answer is {a}."
     "{a}"
     "It is {a}."
     "According to my memory: {a}."
   The answer content is already fixed before theta is used.

   ACTION: Either implement real transient parameter
   steering (e.g., modulate a small decoder's weights)
   or remove the "DNA generates parameters" claim and
   call it "template selection" honestly.

3. compile_from_teacher does not use a teacher.
   The function signature says "teacher" but the actual
   call passes hand-written fact tables:
     dna.compile_from_teacher(facts, aliases=..., clues=...)
   where facts come from world.py hardcoded COUNTRIES
   and ELEMENTS strings.

   ACTION: Implement a real teacher extraction bridge
   using Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct or similar. This is the
   single most important missing piece.

4. Domain B's 47.9x is program compression.
   The "index_range" generative rule stores a formula:
     country = table[idx // 256]
   This is Kolmogorov compression of a generated table,
   not knowledge storage. Calling it "knowledge density"
   is technically misleading.

   ACTION: In all documents, clearly separate:
   (a) fact-storage density (Domain A: 0.42x = LOSS)
   (b) program-compression density (Domain B: 47.9x = WIN)
   These are fundamentally different achievements.

5. No multi-token generation exists.
   The decoder returns one of 4 fixed templates.
   There is no autoregressive generation, no token-level
   decoding, no vocabulary.

   ACTION: Acknowledge this limitation clearly. For the
   current prototype, "answer" means "single entity string
   retrieval", not "language generation".

6. Regex parsing is brittle.
   Queries outside the predefined regex patterns will fail.
   No paraphrase robustness beyond hand-written patterns.

   ACTION: Test with adversarial paraphrases and report
   parse failure rate honestly.

============================================================
SECTION 3: SPECIFIC BUGS TO FIX
============================================================

BUG 1: world.py import name
  run_experiment.py does: import world
  But the file is named world-2.py
  Python cannot import filenames with hyphens.
  FIX: Rename file to world.py

BUG 2: error_types referenced before definition
  In helix.py, _pick_subject and _pick_object:
    except (IndexError, error_types):
  But error_types = re.error is defined at the very
  bottom of the file, after the class.
  FIX: Move to top of file or use re.error directly.

BUG 3: Vocab.decode_vocab is incomplete
  Returns empty Vocab and unmodified offset.
  Currently not called in the main flow, but will
  break if binary-only artifact loading is needed.
  FIX: Implement proper deserialization or remove.

BUG 4: run_experiment.py formatting artifacts
  Some lines appear to have missing operators:
    f"({100.0 res.n_correct/max(1,res.n_queries):.1f}%)"
  Should be:
    f"({100.0 * res.n_correct/max(1,res.n_queries):.1f}%)"
  FIX: Audit all f-strings for missing * operators.

BUG 5: Literal threshold logic is incomplete
  In compile_from_teacher:
    ref = 0xFF00 | (lit & 0xFF) if lit < 0xFF else ...
  But literal_threshold defaults to 0, so this path
  is never taken. And the 0xFF00 sentinel conflicts
  with the OBJ_ENTITY = 0xFFFF sentinel logic.
  FIX: Either implement literals properly or remove.

BUG 6: _h64 signed integer handling
  int.from_bytes(m.digest(), "little") can produce
  negative values in some contexts when used with
  struct.pack("<q", ...) for large hashes.
  FIX: Use unsigned consistently.

============================================================

Do these in order. Do not skip ahead.

PRIORITY 1: REAL TEACHER EXTRACTION BRIDGE
  This is the single most important missing piece.
  Without it, HELIX is a knowledge base, not a DNA system.

  Task:

       HELIX accuracy. The gap = extraction quality loss.

  Success: teacher-extracted accuracy >= 90% of gold-table accuracy.

  a. Implement automatic rule discovery:
     - Given extracted atoms, find inverse relations
     - Find 2-hop chains
     - Find alias closures

     held-out probe gain / description length increase

     instead of a hand-written one.

  b. Retrieved wave state modulates decoder weights:
     W_tilde = W + gamma * A * diag(g(z)) * B^T

  d. This is the actual "DNA thinks" step.

   unless actual wave interference mathematics is implemented.

============================================================
SECTION 7: KEY NUMBERS TO REMEMBER
============================================================

- LLM knowledge density: 2 bits/parameter (Allen-Zhu & Li)
- int8 teacher: 0.25 knowledge bits/byte
- fp16 teacher: 0.125 knowledge bits/byte
- HELIX Domain A: 0.105 bits/stored bit (0.42x vs int8)
- HELIX Domain B: 12.0 bits/stored bit (47.9x vs int8)
- Crossover threshold: D_derive > 3.1 to beat int8 teacher
- 1MB = 8,388,608 bits
- 1 trillion tokens ~ 1.5TB UTF-8 ~ impossible in 1MB
- Current artifact: 12,746 B for 567 atoms
- Bytes per atom (Domain A): ~22.5 B
- Target: <= 10 B/atom after optimization

"""
DECISIVE EXPERIMENT: Real teacher → MDL rule induction → HELIX artifact → DNA-only student
Tests: Is D_derive > 3 reachable in a real domain with automatic compiler?
"""
import os
import sys
import json
import time
import re
from collections import defaultdict, Counter
from typing import List, Tuple, Dict, Optional
import numpy as np

# Add parent directory to path
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))

from helix import KnowledgeDNA, RelSpec
from evaluate import evaluate, tuple_bit_complexity, llm_artifact_bytes

# ============================================================================
# CONFIG
# ============================================================================
N_CONCEPTS = 100  # Start with 100 concepts (scale to 1000 later)
N_RELATIONS = 5   # capital_of, symbol_of, continent_of, country_of, category_of
N_PARAPHRASES = 5 # direct, alias, clue, reverse, chain
USE_REAL_TEACHER = True  # False = mock teacher (fast), True = Qwen 0.5B (slow)

# ============================================================================
# STEP 1: LOAD TEACHER
# ============================================================================
def load_teacher():
    """Load Qwen 0.5B or mock teacher."""
    if not USE_REAL_TEACHER:
        print("⚡ Using MOCK teacher (fast mode)")
        return MockTeacher()
    
    print("🔄 Loading Qwen 0.5B-Instruct (this may take 1-2 minutes on CPU)...")
    from transformers import AutoModelForCausalLM, AutoTokenizer
    
    model_name = "Qwen/Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct"
    tokenizer = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(model_name)
    model = AutoModelForCausalLM.from_pretrained(model_name, torch_dtype="auto", device_map="auto")
    model.eval()
    
    print(f"✅ Teacher loaded: {model_name}")
    return RealTeacher(model, tokenizer)


class MockTeacher:
    """Mock teacher for fast testing (no GPU needed)."""
    def __init__(self):
        # Hardcoded knowledge for 100 countries + 118 elements
        self.knowledge = self._build_knowledge()
    
    def _build_knowledge(self):
        """Build knowledge base from world.py data."""
        import world
        kb = {}
        
        # Countries
        for line in world.COUNTRIES.strip().splitlines():
            parts = line.split("|")
            if len(parts) != 3:
                continue
            country, capital, continent = [p.strip() for p in parts]
            kb[f"{country}|capital_of"] = capital
            kb[f"{country}|continent_of"] = continent
            kb[f"{capital}|country_of"] = country
        
        # Elements
        for i, line in enumerate(world.ELEMENTS.strip().split("|"), start=1):
            parts = line.split(None, 2)
            if len(parts) != 3:
                continue
            symbol, name, category = parts
            kb[f"{name}|symbol_of"] = symbol
            kb[f"{name}|number_of"] = str(i)
            kb[f"{name}|category_of"] = category
        
        return kb
    
    def generate(self, prompt: str, max_new_tokens: int = 8) -> str:
        """Generate answer from knowledge base."""
        prompt_lower = prompt.lower()
        
        # Try to match patterns
        for key, answer in self.knowledge.items():
            entity, relation = key.split("|")
            if entity.lower() in prompt_lower:
                if relation == "capital_of" and "capital" in prompt_lower:
                    return answer
                elif relation == "continent_of" and "continent" in prompt_lower:
                    return answer
                elif relation == "country_of" and "country" in prompt_lower:
                    return answer
                elif relation == "symbol_of" and "symbol" in prompt_lower:
                    return answer
                elif relation == "number_of" and "number" in prompt_lower:
                    return answer
                elif relation == "category_of" and "category" in prompt_lower:
                    return answer
        
        return "I don't know."


class RealTeacher:
    """Real Qwen 0.5B teacher."""
    def __init__(self, model, tokenizer):
        self.model = model
        self.tokenizer = tokenizer
    
    def generate(self, prompt: str, max_new_tokens: int = 8) -> str:
        """Generate answer using Qwen 0.5B."""
        inputs = self.tokenizer(prompt, return_tensors="pt").to(self.model.device)
        with torch.no_grad():
            outputs = self.model.generate(
                **inputs,
                max_new_tokens=max_new_tokens,
                do_sample=False,
                pad_token_id=self.tokenizer.eos_token_id
            )
        answer = self.tokenizer.decode(outputs[0][inputs.input_ids.shape[1]:], skip_special_tokens=True)
        return answer.strip()


# ============================================================================
# STEP 2: GENERATE PROBE GRID
# ============================================================================
def generate_probe_grid(concepts: List[str]) -> List[Tuple[str, str, str]]:
    """
    Generate probe grid: (prompt, entity, relation)
    100 concepts × 5 relations × 5 paraphrases = 2500 probes
    """
    probes = []
    
    # Import world data for aliases and clues
    import world
    
    for concept in concepts:
        # Determine if country or element
        is_country = concept in [line.split("|")[0].strip() for line in world.COUNTRIES.strip().splitlines()]
        
        if is_country:
            # Country relations
            relations = ["capital_of", "continent_of"]
        else:
            # Element relations
            relations = ["symbol_of", "number_of", "category_of"]
        
        for relation in relations:
            # Generate 5 paraphrases
            paraphrases = generate_paraphrases(concept, relation, world.ALIASES, world.CLUES)
            for prompt in paraphrases:
                probes.append((prompt, concept, relation))
    
    return probes


def generate_paraphrases(entity: str, relation: str, aliases: Dict[str, str], clues: Dict[str, str]) -> List[str]:
    """Generate 5 paraphrases for a given entity-relation pair."""
    paraphrases = []
    
    # Find alias for this entity
    alias = None
    for a, canon in aliases.items():
        if canon == entity:
            alias = a
            break
    
    # Find clue for this entity
    clue = None
    for c, canon in clues.items():
        if canon == entity:
            clue = c
            break
    
    if relation == "capital_of":
        paraphrases = [
            f"What is the capital of {entity}?",  # direct
            f"What is the capital of {alias}?" if alias else f"What is the capital of {entity}?",  # alias
            f"What is the capital of the {clue} country?" if clue else f"What is the capital of {entity}?",  # clue
            f"Which country has {entity}'s capital as its capital?",  # reverse (broken, will fail)
            f"What is the capital of the country of {entity}'s capital?",  # chain (2-hop)
        ]
    elif relation == "continent_of":
        paraphrases = [
            f"What continent is {entity} in?",
            f"What continent is {alias} in?" if alias else f"What continent is {entity} in?",
            f"What continent is the {clue} country in?" if clue else f"What continent is {entity} in?",
            f"Which country is in the same continent as {entity}?",  # reverse (broken)
            f"What continent is the country of {entity} in?",  # chain
        ]
    elif relation == "symbol_of":
        paraphrases = [
            f"What is the chemical symbol of {entity}?",
            f"What is the symbol of {alias}?" if alias else f"What is the symbol of {entity}?",
            f"What is the symbol of the {clue} element?" if clue else f"What is the symbol of {entity}?",
            f"Which element has the symbol {entity}?",  # reverse
            f"What is the symbol of the element {entity}?",  # chain
        ]
    elif relation == "number_of":
        paraphrases = [
            f"What is the atomic number of {entity}?",
            f"What is the number of {alias}?" if alias else f"What is the number of {entity}?",
            f"What is the atomic number of the {clue} element?" if clue else f"What is the atomic number of {entity}?",
            f"Which element has atomic number {entity}?",  # reverse
            f"What is the atomic number of {entity}?",  # chain
        ]
    elif relation == "category_of":
        paraphrases = [
            f"What category of element is {entity}?",
            f"What category is {alias}?" if alias else f"What category is {entity}?",
            f"What category is the {clue} element?" if clue else f"What category is {entity}?",
            f"Which elements are {entity}?",  # reverse
            f"What category does {entity} belong to?",  # chain
        ]
    
    return paraphrases[:N_PARAPHRASES]


# ============================================================================
# STEP 3: EXTRACT ATOMS FROM TEACHER
# ============================================================================
def extract_atoms(teacher, probes: List[Tuple[str, str, str]]) -> Dict[Tuple[str, str], str]:
    """
    Extract atoms from teacher: (entity, relation) → answer
    Group probes by (entity, relation), take modal answer.
    """
    print(f"\n🔬 Extracting atoms from {len(probes)} probes...")
    
    buckets = defaultdict(list)
    
    for i, (prompt, entity, relation) in enumerate(probes):
        if (i + 1) % 100 == 0:
            print(f"  Progress: {i + 1}/{len(probes)}")
        
        answer = teacher.generate(prompt, max_new_tokens=8)
        buckets[(entity, relation)].append(answer)
    
    # Take modal answer for each (entity, relation)
    atoms = {}
    for (entity, relation), answers in buckets.items():
        if len(answers) < 2:
            continue  # Need at least 2 answers for modal
        
        # Find most common answer
        counter = Counter(answers)
        modal_answer, count = counter.most_common(1)[0]
        
        # Only keep if at least 50% agreement
        if count >= len(answers) * 0.5:
            atoms[(entity, relation)] = modal_answer
    
    print(f"✅ Extracted {len(atoms)} atoms (from {len(buckets)} buckets)")
    return atoms


# ============================================================================
# STEP 4: MDL RULE INDUCTION (GREEDY)
# ============================================================================
def induce_rules(atoms: Dict[Tuple[str, str], str]) -> Dict:
    """
    Greedy MDL rule induction:
    1. Find inverse relations
    2. Find 2-hop chains
    3. Find alias closure
    """
    print(f"\n🧠 Inducing rules from {len(atoms)} atoms...")
    
    rules = {
        "inverse": {},
        "chain": {},
        "alias": {}
    }
    
    # Step 1: Find inverse relations
    # If (A, r1) → B and (B, r2) → A, then r2 = inverse(r1)
    print("  Step 1: Finding inverse relations...")
    for (e1, r1), o1 in atoms.items():
        for (e2, r2), o2 in atoms.items():
            if e1 == o2 and e2 == o1 and r1 != r2:
                # Found inverse
                if r1 not in rules["inverse"]:
                    rules["inverse"][r1] = r2
                    print(f"    Found: {r2} is inverse of {r1}")
    
    # Step 2: Find 2-hop chains
    # If (A, r1) → B and (B, r2) → C, then (A, r1∘r2) → C
    print("  Step 2: Finding 2-hop chains...")
    chain_count = 0
    for (e1, r1), o1 in atoms.items():
        for (e2, r2), o2 in atoms.items():
            if o1 == e2:
                # Found chain: e1 -r1-> o1=e2 -r2-> o2
                chain_rel = f"{r1}_then_{r2}"
                rules["chain"][(e1, chain_rel)] = o2
                chain_count += 1
    
    print(f"    Found {chain_count} 2-hop chains")
    
    # Step 3: Find alias closure
    # If alias(A) = B and (B, r) → C, then (A, r) → C
    print("  Step 3: Finding alias closure...")
    import world
    alias_count = 0
    for alias, canon in world.ALIASES.items():
        for (e, r), o in atoms.items():
            if e == canon:
                rules["alias"][(alias, r)] = o
                alias_count += 1
    
    print(f"    Found {alias_count} alias closures")
    
    print(f"✅ Induced rules: {len(rules['inverse'])} inverses, {len(rules['chain'])} chains, {len(rules['alias'])} aliases")
    return rules


# ============================================================================
# STEP 5: PACK ARTIFACT
# ============================================================================
def pack_artifact(atoms: Dict[Tuple[str, str], str], rules: Dict) -> KnowledgeDNA:
    """Pack atoms + rules into HELIX artifact."""
    print(f"\n📦 Packing artifact...")
    
    # Convert atoms to facts format
    facts = [(e, r, o) for (e, r), o in atoms.items()]
    
    # Import world data
    import world
    
    # Create DNA
    dna = KnowledgeDNA()
    
    # Add relation specs
    for rel_name in set(r for (_, r) in atoms.keys()):
        dna.rules.add_rel(RelSpec(rel_name))
    
    # Add inverse relations
    for r1, r2 in rules["inverse"].items():
        if r1 in dna.rules.rels:
            dna.rules.inverse_of[r2] = r1
    
    # Add aliases
    for alias, canon in world.ALIASES.items():
        dna.rules.aliases[alias.lower()] = canon
    
    # Add clues
    for clue, canon in world.CLUES.items():
        dna.rules.clues[clue.lower()] = canon
    
    # Compile
    dna.compile_from_teacher(facts, aliases=world.ALIASES, clues=world.CLUES, rels=world.REL_SPECS)
    
    # Save artifact
    artifact_path = "artifacts/decisive_experiment.dna"
    os.makedirs("artifacts", exist_ok=True)
    dna.save(artifact_path)
    
    artifact_size = os.path.getsize(artifact_path)
    print(f"✅ Artifact saved: {artifact_path} ({artifact_size:,} bytes)")
    
    return dna


# ============================================================================
# STEP 6: RUN DNA-ONLY STUDENT
# ============================================================================
def run_dna_student(dna: KnowledgeDNA, probes: List[Tuple[str, str, str]]) -> Dict:
    """Run DNA-only student on probes and measure metrics."""
    print(f"\n🧪 Running DNA-only student on {len(probes)} probes...")
    
    correct = 0
    total = 0
    unknown_correct = 0
    unknown_total = 0
    
    for i, (prompt, entity, relation) in enumerate(probes):
        if (i + 1) % 100 == 0:
            print(f"  Progress: {i + 1}/{len(probes)}")
        
        # Encode query
        parsed = dna.encode_query(prompt)
        
        # Retrieve
        ret = dna.retrieve(parsed)
        
        # Check if unknown
        if ret.answer is None:
            unknown_total += 1
            # For now, we don't have ground truth for unknowns
            # In real experiment, we'd add unknown probes
            continue
        
        # Check answer
        total += 1
        if ret.answer.lower().strip() == entity.lower().strip():
            correct += 1
    
    accuracy = correct / max(1, total)
    unknown_accuracy = unknown_correct / max(1, unknown_total)
    
    print(f"✅ DNA-only student: {correct}/{total} correct ({accuracy:.1%})")
    
    return {
        "accuracy": accuracy,
        "correct": correct,
        "total": total,
        "unknown_accuracy": unknown_accuracy,
        "unknown_correct": unknown_correct,
        "unknown_total": unknown_total
    }


# ============================================================================
# STEP 7: COMPUTE METRICS
# ============================================================================
def compute_metrics(dna: KnowledgeDNA, atoms: Dict, rules: Dict, student_metrics: Dict) -> Dict:
    """Compute final metrics: D_derive, bits/answer, etc."""
    print(f"\n📊 Computing metrics...")
    
    # Count stored atoms
    n_atoms = len(atoms)
    
    # Count answerable tuples (atoms + derived)
    n_tuples = n_atoms
    n_tuples += len(rules["inverse"])  # Inverse relations
    n_tuples += len(rules["chain"])     # 2-hop chains
    n_tuples += len(rules["alias"])     # Alias closures
    
    # Compute D_derive
    D_derive = n_tuples / max(1, n_atoms)
    
    # Compute artifact size
    artifact_path = "artifacts/decisive_experiment.dna"
    artifact_size = os.path.getsize(artifact_path)
    
    # Compute bits per answer
    bits_per_answer = (artifact_size * 8) / max(1, n_tuples)
    
    # Compute bits per stored bit
    knowledge_bits = tuple_bit_complexity([(e, r, o) for (e, r), o in atoms.items()], len(dna.rules.rels)) * n_tuples
    stored_bits = artifact_size * 8
    bits_per_stored_bit = knowledge_bits / max(1, stored_bits)
    
    # Compare to int8 teacher
    int8_bits_per_param = 0.25
    int8_teacher_size = llm_artifact_bytes(knowledge_bits, 8)
    density_vs_int8 = bits_per_stored_bit / int8_bits_per_param
    
    metrics = {
        "n_atoms": n_atoms,
        "n_tuples": n_tuples,
        "D_derive": D_derive,
        "artifact_size": artifact_size,
        "bits_per_answer": bits_per_answer,
        "bits_per_stored_bit": bits_per_stored_bit,
        "density_vs_int8": density_vs_int8,
        "student_accuracy": student_metrics["accuracy"],
        "student_correct": student_metrics["correct"],
        "student_total": student_metrics["total"],
    }
    
    print(f"✅ Metrics computed:")
    print(f"  Stored atoms: {n_atoms}")
    print(f"  Answerable tuples: {n_tuples}")
    print(f"  D_derive: {D_derive:.2f}")
    print(f"  Artifact size: {artifact_size:,} bytes")
    print(f"  Bits per answer: {bits_per_answer:.2f}")
    print(f"  Bits per stored bit: {bits_per_stored_bit:.3f}")
    print(f"  Density vs int8 teacher: {density_vs_int8:.2f}x")
    print(f"  Student accuracy: {student_metrics['accuracy']:.1%}")
    
    return metrics


# ============================================================================
# STEP 8: DECISION GATE
# ============================================================================
def decision_gate(metrics: Dict) -> bool:
    """Check if experiment passes decision gate."""
    print(f"\n🎯 Decision gate check...")
    
    # Gate 1: D_derive > 3
    if metrics["D_derive"] < 3.0:
        print(f"  ❌ FAIL: D_derive = {metrics['D_derive']:.2f} < 3.0")
        return False
    print(f"  ✅ PASS: D_derive = {metrics['D_derive']:.2f} > 3.0")
    
    # Gate 2: Student accuracy > 95%
    if metrics["student_accuracy"] < 0.95:
        print(f"  ❌ FAIL: Student accuracy = {metrics['student_accuracy']:.1%} < 95%")
        return False
    print(f"  ✅ PASS: Student accuracy = {metrics['student_accuracy']:.1%} > 95%")
    
    # Gate 3: Density vs int8 > 1.0x
    if metrics["density_vs_int8"] < 1.0:
        print(f"  ❌ FAIL: Density vs int8 = {metrics['density_vs_int8']:.2f}x < 1.0x")
        return False
    print(f"  ✅ PASS: Density vs int8 = {metrics['density_vs_int8']:.2f}x > 1.0x")
    
    print(f"\n🎉 ALL GATES PASSED! Experiment successful.")
    return True


# ============================================================================
# MAIN
# ============================================================================
def main():
    print("=" * 80)
    print("DECISIVE EXPERIMENT: Real teacher → MDL rule induction → HELIX artifact")
    print("=" * 80)
    
    start_time = time.time()
    
    # Step 1: Load teacher
    teacher = load_teacher()
    
    # Step 2: Generate probe grid
    import world
    concepts = [line.split("|")[0].strip() for line in world.COUNTRIES.strip().splitlines()][:N_CONCEPTS]
    concepts += [line.split(None, 2)[1] for line in world.ELEMENTS.strip().split("|")][:N_CONCEPTS]
    concepts = concepts[:N_CONCEPTS]  # Limit to N_CONCEPTS
    
    probes = generate_probe_grid(concepts)
    print(f"\n📋 Generated {len(probes)} probes for {len(concepts)} concepts")
    
    # Step 3: Extract atoms
    atoms = extract_atoms(teacher, probes)
    
    # Step 4: Induce rules
    rules = induce_rules(atoms)
    
    # Step 5: Pack artifact
    dna = pack_artifact(atoms, rules)
    
    # Step 6: Run DNA-only student
    student_metrics = run_dna_student(dna, probes)
    
    # Step 7: Compute metrics
    metrics = compute_metrics(dna, atoms, rules, student_metrics)
    
    # Step 8: Decision gate
    passed = decision_gate(metrics)
    
    # Save results
    results = {
        "metrics": metrics,
        "rules": {
            "n_inverse": len(rules["inverse"]),
            "n_chain": len(rules["chain"]),
            "n_alias": len(rules["alias"])
        },
        "passed": passed,
        "elapsed_time": time.time() - start_time
    }
    
    with open("artifacts/decisive_experiment_results.json", "w") as f:
        json.dump(results, f, indent=2)
    
    print(f"\n💾 Results saved to artifacts/decisive_experiment_results.json")
    print(f"⏱️ Total time: {results['elapsed_time']:.1f}s")
    
    if passed:
        print("\n🎉 SUCCESS: D_derive > 3 is reachable with automatic compiler!")
        print("   Next: Scale to 1000 concepts, add interference injection, write paper.")
    else:
        print("\n❌ FAILURE: D_derive > 3 not reachable with current approach.")
        print("   Next: Debug rule induction, try different domains, or accept limitation.")
    
    return passed

    import torch  # For RealTeacher
    main()You are acting as an elite AI research scientist, neural architecture designer, information theorist, LLM compression engineer, and startup CTO.

Your task is NOT to give a generic answer.
Your task is to design a new parametric knowledge-storage architecture that can store dramatically more usable knowledge per parameter than ordinary neural network weights.

============================================================
PROJECT CONTEXT: REVERSE DNA
============================================================

We are working on a project called Reverse DNA.

Original dream:
Take a pretrained LLM's knowledge/behavior and extract it into a very small "DNA" artifact, ideally around 1MB, such that:

    Input prompt
       +
    DNA artifact
       ↓
    DNA generates temporary parameters / coefficients / memory states
       ↓
    Those parameters think / retrieve / generate answer
       ↓
    Output behaves like the teacher LLM for a target knowledge domain.

Important:
We do NOT want merely:
- quantization
- pruning
- distillation
- LoRA
- normal SVD compression
- activation hooks on top of the base model

Those are known and insufficient for the core problem.

The real problem is:
How can a tiny number of parameters store a huge amount of usable knowledge?

============================================================
WHAT WE HAVE ALREADY LEARNED FROM EXPERIMENTS
============================================================

We experimented heavily with Qwen2.5-0.5B-Instruct and related models.

Key findings:

1. Raw weight compression is weak.
   - Fourier weight-coordinate DNA failed.
   - Real LLM weights do not behave like smooth coordinate functions.
   - Weight-space compression did not preserve behavior well.

2. Activation-space knowledge is more promising.
   - Teacher knowledge appears more accessible through hidden states, deltas, logits, routing patterns, and output sensitivity.
   - We extracted "knowledge waves" from activation space.
   - A concept like "Paris" is not one scalar; it is a distributed activation pattern across layers and dimensions.

3. Flat low-rank SVD causes identity collapse.
   - Rank-16 SVD preserved high-variance grammar/template directions.
   - But it destroyed low-variance entity identity directions.
   - Example:
       Japan vs Mexico original hidden distance = 0.1119
       After flat Rank-16 DNA distance = 0.0001
       Ratio = 0.001
   - This means blind variance compression is not behavior compression.

4. Hierarchical/Spiral residual SVD was not magically better at equal rank.
   - We mathematically tested flat Rank-R vs sequential residual SVD.
   - At equal total rank, they were numerically almost equivalent.
   - Therefore "Spiral Zoom beats flat SVD" is not proven.
   - The real value is not the Spiral branding, but the idea that knowledge may be wave/function/compositional.

5. DNA-only retrieval prototype worked for stored concepts.
   - We built prototypes where teacher model was used only for extraction, then deleted.
   - Inference used only DNA arrays.
   - For stored concepts, DNA could retrieve answers from:
       direct prompts
       alias prompts
       clue prompts
       reverse prompts
       distractor prompts
   - It also showed low confidence for unknown concepts.

6. But current DNA is still limited.
   - It works mainly as a hybrid retrieval cartridge:
       activation wave memory
       + masked hash memory
       + symbolic n-gram index
       + small fusion router
   - It does NOT yet prove that a tiny DNA can act as a full thinking model.
   - It does NOT yet solve the fundamental knowledge-density problem.

============================================================
THE CORE UNSOLVED PROBLEM
============================================================

Ordinary neural network parameters are extremely inefficient as explicit knowledge storage.

Example intuition:
A single scalar parameter may carry only a tiny amount of stable information.
If one parameter stores roughly 16-32 bits of usable information, then:

    1MB = 8,388,608 bits

Even if one fact requires only 20-50 bits, ordinary parameter storage gives a rough upper bound of hundreds of thousands to a few million simple facts, assuming perfect addressing and zero redundancy.

But real knowledge is not isolated facts.
It requires:
- entity identity
- relations
- paraphrase robustness
- aliases
- reverse queries
- context sensitivity
- uncertainty
- refusal on unknown
- multi-token generation
- compositional reasoning

Therefore the central question is:

Can we design a new parameter architecture where each parameter participates in many overlapping knowledge patterns, like a hologram, wavefield, program, or dynamical system, so that the same 1MB stores 10x, 100x, 1000x, or more usable knowledge than ordinary weight storage?

This is the main problem to solve.

============================================================
WHAT I WANT YOU TO DESIGN
============================================================

Design a new architecture called something like:

    High-Density Parametric Knowledge Memory
    or
    DNA Parametric Mind Architecture
    or
    Knowledge Wavefield Memory

But do not just give a name. Give a real mathematical and engineering design.

The architecture must satisfy these constraints:

1. Tiny artifact target:
   - Start with 1MB DNA artifact.
   - Later scale to 10MB.
   - The artifact must be self-contained for a narrow/domain knowledge brain.
   - It may be compiled from a teacher LLM, but at inference the teacher LLM must not be required.

2. No external giant database:
   - Do not solve the problem by saying "store facts in an external vector DB".
   - The knowledge must be represented inside the DNA parameters/functions themselves.
   - Small indexes or hashes can be part of the design, but the core innovation must be parametric knowledge density.

3. Must support usable knowledge:
   - entity retrieval
   - alias resolution

   - reverse queries

       France -> Paris
